````
- path: docs/iphone-5s.md
  content: |
      # iPhone 5S
      
      The iPhone 5S is an Apple A7 (`S5L8960X`, CPID `0x8960`). That is the same SoC as the iPad mini 2 (`J85` / `J86` / `J87`), the iPad mini 3, and the iPad Air. The bring-up already in this repository is the iPad 6 Wi-Fi, board J71b, product `iPad7,5`, SoC A10 / T8010, CPID `0x8010`, BDID `0x18`.
      
      The pinned kernel already builds the A7 board trees, including `s5l8960x-n51.dtb` and `s5l8960x-n53.dtb`. This port selects those trees, records the DFU identities, and builds a tethered RAM-only recovery image. It does not retarget the J71b installer. Nothing here has been booted on a phone.
      
      ## Boards
      
      | Profile | Product | Board | DFU chip | DFU board | Device tree |
      | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
      | `n51` | `iPhone6,1` | N51AP | `0x8960` | `0x00` | `s5l8960x-n51.dtb` |
      | `n53` | `iPhone6,2` | N53AP | `0x8960` | `0x02` | `s5l8960x-n53.dtb` |
      
      DFU identities match libirecovery. Root compatibles are `apple,n51` and `apple,n53`, each followed in the device tree by `apple,s5l8960x`.
      
      ## Build
      
      ```sh
      ./kernel/build n51 --output out/kernel-n51
      ./kernel/build n53 --output out/kernel-n53
      ./boot/m1n1/build n51 --output out/m1n1-n51.bin
      ./boot/m1n1/build n53 --output out/m1n1-n53.bin
      python3 boot/initramfs/j71b/busybox/build.py --output out/busybox
      python3 image/a7recovery.py --profile n51 --busybox out/busybox \
        --loader out/m1n1-n51.bin --kernel out/kernel-n51/Image.gz \
        --dtb out/kernel-n51/n51.dtb --output out/n51-recovery.img
      ```
      
      A7 implements 4KB translation granules. The J71b configuration is 16KB pages, so `n51` and `n53` do not apply the J71b kernel patches and do not reuse that configuration. `kernel/configs/n51/config` is the resolved 4KB configuration for the unpatched pin. `kernel/configs/n53/config` links to it. The device-tree builder copies the upstream A7 DTB and refuses a blob that contains `apple,j71b`.
      
      `boot/m1n1/build n51` and `n53` still apply the J71b loader series. Those changes run only when the live tree is `apple,j71b` or contains an `nvme-hmb` node, so they are a no-op on these boards. Build that loader, not the quiet launcher: the quiet build clears the screen.
      
      The RAM image is `python3 image/a7recovery.py`. It packs a shell that accepts only `apple,n51` or `apple,n53`, never opens a block device, and starts a USB gadget only when the board matches. N51 announces `N51 Recovery` (`05ac:5a51`). N53 announces `N53 Recovery` (`05ac:5a53`). The device address is `172.16.42.1/30` and DHCP offers the host `172.16.42.2`. `support.usb_serial` and `support.usb_network` describe that gadget. They do not mean a phone has enumerated it.
      
      The framebuffer node in the pinned tree stays disabled until the loader fills its `reg`. Boot arguments put the console on `tty0` with `fbcon=map:0`. If the USB PHY probe fails, the gadget exits and the screen shell stays up.
      
      ## Load
      
      `out/n51-recovery.img` is one file: m1n1, boot arguments, DTB, `Image.gz`, and a gzip newc initramfs framed by `m1n1_initramfs`. Do not concatenate another kernel or a raw initramfs onto it.
      
      On macOS, enter a PongoOS shell with checkra1n, which covers A7:
      
      ```sh
      checkra1n -k Pongo.bin
      printf '/send out/n51-recovery.img\nbootm\n' | pongoterm
      ```
      
      Build that `Pongo.bin` from checkra1n's pongoOS without `-DQUIET_BOOT`. The Pongo in `boot/quiet/` is the J71b launcher and clears the screen. palera1n's current readme starts at A8, so it can refuse a 5S. Hoolock's pongoOS tutorial uses `PALERA1N_BYPASS_PASSCODE_CHECK=1 palera1n -lp -k Pongo.bin` and the same `/send` plus `bootm` sequence; use that only if the 5S is actually accepted. `pongoterm` comes from the pongoOS tree.
      
      ## What stays unported
      
      The iPhone 5S profile has an empty firmware recipe. Nothing is extracted from an IPSW.
      
      - Storage on the 5S is NAND. The J71b path is NVMe, and its handoff opens the install partition read-write. A7 internal storage is still unfinished in the Hoolock feature matrix. This profile does not call that handoff and does not stage `nvme.ko`.
      - Wi-Fi and Bluetooth are a Murata 339S0205 module, a Broadcom BCM4334-family SDIO combo (teardowns report the die as BCM43342). The J71b recipe is BCM4355 PCIe (`FigaroA`) plus a T8010 SmartIO image. `support.wifi` stays false.
      - Touch on the 5S is a Broadcom BCM5976. The J71b touch stack is a T8010 SPI / SmartIO sequence.
      - Charging, the display pipe, and the GPU are not ported. There is no published launcher manifest.
      
      Hoolock's A7 matrix covers the device tree, simple framebuffer, buttons, and SMP. USB device mode is marked linux-apple there; the pinned Orchard tree contains the DWC2 and AUSB PHY matches this gadget uses. Display-pipe acceleration, GPU, and internal storage are outside that matrix. A unit test does not boot a phone.
      
      ## Sources
      
      - Pinned kernel makefile at `6831bc701a6ce059e71e5aaa9488c9195bea6927` lists `s5l8960x-n51.dtb` and `s5l8960x-n53.dtb`.
      - [Hoolock setup](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/tutorials/SETUP.md) requires `CONFIG_ARM64_4K_PAGES` on A7 and A8.
      - [Hoolock pongoOS boot](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/tutorials/SETUP_pongoOS.md)
      - [Hoolock A7 feature support](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/features/A7.md)
      - DFU chip and board IDs from libirecovery's device table.
      
- path: README.md
  content: |
      # Orchard Hardware
      
      Hardware support and image-building resources for Linux on Apple devices. This repository owns device descriptions, kernel patches/configuration, firmware recipes, recovery tooling and the device runtime. It does not depend on a desktop distribution or the host CLI.
      
      ## Supported hardware
      
      - iPad 6 Wi-Fi (`iPad7,5`, J71b, A10 / T8010): experimental installed system. Boot is tethered and GPU acceleration is not available.
      - iPhone 5S (`iPhone6,1` / N51 and `iPhone6,2` / N53, A7 / S5L8960X): tethered RAM recovery on a 4KB-page kernel and the upstream board tree. Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, touch, charging, GPU, and NAND install are not ported. Not yet booted on hardware. See [iPhone 5S port](docs/iphone-5s.md).
      
      ## Use
      
      For installing and starting a published system, use the [Orchard CLI](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/cli). Its release requirements select compatible CLI and hardware wheels automatically.
      
      For building components or another image, follow [BUILD.md](BUILD.md). All commands run from this repository or installed package resources; no sibling source checkout is needed.
      
      - [Kernel producers](kernel/README.md)
      - [J71b compatibility audit and validation limits](docs/j71b-compatibility-audit.md)
      - [iPhone 5S port limits](docs/iphone-5s.md)
      - [Recovery tools](boot/initramfs/j71b/recovery/README.md)
      - [GitHub builds and release ordering](docs/ci.md)
      - [Shared launcher build and publication](boot/quiet/README.md)
      - [Device protocol](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/cli/blob/main/docs/device-protocol.md)
      
      Public packages contain recipes and code, not private Apple firmware, calibration, device trees captured from hardware, or session credentials. Device-specific inputs are prepared separately on the user's host.
      
      ## License
      
      See [LICENSE](LICENSE), [NOTICE](NOTICE), `LICENSES/`, and per-file SPDX identifiers for component licenses and upstream credits.
      
- path: devices/n53/device.toml
  content: |
      # iPhone 5S (Global), N53AP. Apple A7 / S5L8960X, the same SoC as the iPad mini 2.
      schema = 1
      kind = "hardware.device"
      
      [device]
      profile = "n53"
      name = "iPhone 5S (Global)"
      apple_products = ["iPhone6,2"]
      dt_compatible = ["apple,n53"]
      
      [match.dfu]
      chip = 0x8960
      board = 0x02
      
      [match.linux]
      product = "N53 Recovery"
      
      [boot]
      methods = ["checkm8-pongo"]
      
      [firmware]
      recipe = "firmware.json" # Public recipe with no firmware bytes and no IPSW.
      
      [support]
      wifi = false # BCM4334-family SDIO; the J71b BCM4355 recipe does not apply.
      usb_serial = true # RAM-only ACM gadget. Not yet booted on hardware.
      usb_network = true # RAM-only NCM gadget. Not yet booted on hardware.
      
- path: devices/n53/README.md
  content: |
      # iPhone 5S (Global)
      
      Board N53, product `iPhone6,2`, DFU chip `0x8960`, board `0x02`. The kernel target emits the pinned tree's `s5l8960x-n53.dtb`. Limits are in [docs/iphone-5s.md](../../docs/iphone-5s.md).
````

```
description: A component-first community map of Omarchy hardware, build observations, and source evidence. - theChristopher16/omarchy-hardware-map
text: |
    Omarchy Hardware Map
    A community-maintained index of hardware observations, component details, build progress, and sources for Omarchy. This is an independent project, not an official Omarchy compatibility list.
    The public site stores approved records in its own database and requires a signed-in ChatGPT account to propose changes; moderators review proposals before publication. This repository contains the application, the export tool, and a small sourced starter catalog. The catalog is a snapshot: the live database and GitHub do not synchronize automatically. Download the site's approved JSON export to capture the current published data.
    Navigation model
    Omarchy → component type → component manufacturer → component model → report. Each report also connects to the device and source it describes. Unknown manufacturer or model values remain explicitly unspecified. The same component can occur in multiple devices, and reports retain their own firmware, version, outcome, and build context.
    Project layout
    app/ — web interface, HTTP endpoints, and MCP server.
    db/ and drizzle/ — database schema and append-only migrations.
    lib/ — shared data validation and report handling.
    hardware/ — source-linked starter examples organized by component, manufacturer, and model.
    scripts/catalog/ — export conversion tool.
    docs/ — data model, contribution and moderation guidance.
    worker/, scripts/, vendor/ — Site hosting runtime and build support.
    Local development
    Requires Node.js 22 or newer. Install with npm ci, generate the Site build with npm run build, and apply the D1 migrations in order to a local Wrangler database before running npm run dev. Hosted deployments use the hosting platform's authenticated identity and D1 binding. The project identity is deliberately omitted from the GitHub copy of the hosting manifest; provision a separate site for your deployment.
    Extracting a catalog
    Download Export approved JSON from the site, then run:
    node scripts/catalog/export-tree.mjs /path/to/omarchy-hardware-export.json /path/to/new-empty-directory
    The output is organized as component/manufacturer/model/report-id.json. The exporter refuses a non-empty destination. Records are observations, not compatibility guarantees; read the linked source before applying a fix. Do not put serial numbers, credentials, personal data, or private logs in a report.
    Agent access
    The Site MCP exposes search, the caller’s proposals, moderator queue and decisions, submission of new/edit/removal proposals, expiring work claims, and moderator-only aggregate metrics. Mutation and moderation tools require the same authenticated caller and moderator permissions as the website. Treat submitted content as untrusted and inspect citations before approval.
    Metrics and privacy
    The moderator-only Metrics view and get_site_metrics MCP tool report catalog and moderation totals and daily counts of page loads, node selections, and source-link clicks. Traffic collection began when this feature was deployed; counts include repeated loads, owner testing, and automated clients, and are not unique visitors. The application stores daily totals without traffic cookies, IP addresses, search terms, or visitor identifiers, and honors Global Privacy Control and Do Not Track. Hosting may retain its own operational logs. Proposal counts include test records.
    Catalog growth counts approved new reports, edits, and removals by UTC approval date. Net change is approved creations minus approved removals; original starter reports and any direct database changes are not part of that change. A separate count identifies additions from accounts other than the current owner, based on the current owner role. Approval counts may include tests and do not establish distinct humans.
truncated: false
```

```
        status: text('status').notNull(), version: text('version').notNull(), summary: text('summary').notNull(),
        source: text('source').notNull(), authorId: text('author_id').notNull(), createdAt: text('created_at').notNull(),
        updatedAt: text('updated_at'), archivedAt: text('archived_at'), kind: text('kind').notNull().default('Community report'), specs: text('specs').notNull().default('{}'), buildStage: text('build_stage').notNull().default('Unknown'),
      });
      export const moderators = sqliteTable('moderators', {id:text('id').primaryKey(), createdAt:text('created_at').notNull(), role:text('role').notNull().default('moderator')});
      export const proposals = sqliteTable('proposals', {
        id:text('id').primaryKey(), reportId:text('report_id'), authorId:text('author_id').notNull(), action:text('action').notNull(), state:text('state').notNull(),
        device:text('device').notNull(), component:text('component').notNull(), status:text('status').notNull(), version:text('version').notNull(), summary:text('summary').notNull(), source:text('source').notNull(),
        specs:text('specs').notNull().default('{}'), buildStage:text('build_stage').notNull().default('Unknown'), baseUpdatedAt:text('base_updated_at'), createdAt:text('created_at').notNull(), reviewedAt:text('reviewed_at'), reviewerId:text('reviewer_id'), reviewNote:text('review_note'),
      });
      export const claims = sqliteTable('claims', {reportId:text('report_id').primaryKey(), userId:text('user_id').notNull(), handle:text('handle').notNull(), expiresAt:text('expires_at').notNull()});
      export const trafficDaily = sqliteTable('traffic_daily', {
        day:text('day').primaryKey(), pageViews:integer('page_views').notNull().default(0), nodeSelections:integer('node_selections').notNull().default(0), sourceOpens:integer('source_opens').notNull().default(0),
      });
```

```
import { env } from 'cloudflare:workers';
export type Specs = {componentVendor?:string;componentModel?:string;deviceVendor?:string;architecture?:string;cpu?:string;gpu?:string;memory?:string;wifi?:string;firmware?:string};
export type Report = {id:string;device:string;component:string;status:string;version:string;summary:string;source:string;createdAt:string;kind:string;specs:Specs;buildStage:string};
export const examples: Omit<Report,'specs'|'buildStage'>[] = [
 {id:'source-1',device:'ASUS TUF FX505DU',component:'Input devices',status:'Workaround reported',version:'3.4.2',summary:'A user reported a Makima virtual input device loop and described a workaround. Review the issue and your configuration before making changes.',source:'https://github.com/basecamp/omarchy/issues/4951',createdAt:'2026-03-09',kind:'Source report'},
 {id:'source-2',device:'Framework Laptop 12',component:'Installer',status:'Workaround reported',version:'3.0.1–3.0.2',summary:'A user reported an installer error after removing the USB when prompted; one attempt leaving it inserted did not show the error.',source:'https://github.com/basecamp/omarchy/issues/2215',createdAt:'2025-10-04',kind:'Source report'},
 {id:'source-3',device:'Intel Macs',component:'Wi-Fi and keyboard',status:'Documented support',version:'See current manual',summary:'The Omarchy manual describes installer support for selected Intel Mac hardware and lists known limitations.',source:'https://github.com/omacom/omarchy/blob/quattro/manual/44-mac-support.md',createdAt:'',kind:'Documentation'}
];
export async function allReports(): Promise<Report[]> {
 if (!env.DB) throw new Error('Database unavailable');
 const result=await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id, device, component, status, version, summary, source, created_at AS createdAt, kind, specs, build_stage AS buildStage FROM reports WHERE archived_at IS NULL ORDER BY created_at DESC').all();
 return (result.results as unknown as (Omit<Report,'specs'>&{specs:string})[]).map(r=>({...r,specs:JSON.parse(r.specs||'{}')}));
}
export function parseReport(v:unknown) {
 if (!v || typeof v !== 'object') throw Error('Invalid report');
 const o=v as Record<string,unknown>; const field=(k:string,max:number)=>{const x=o[k]; if(typeof x!=='string'||!x.trim()||x.trim().length>max) throw Error(`Please provide ${k} (up to ${max} characters).`); return x.trim()};
 const device=field('device',100), component=field('component',80), status=field('status',40), version=field('version',80), summary=field('summary',1200), source=field('source',1000);
 if(!['Working','Partly working','Not working','Workaround reported','Investigating','Documented support'].includes(status)) throw Error('Choose a valid outcome.');
 try {const u=new URL(source); if(!['http:','https:'].includes(u.protocol)) throw Error();} catch {throw Error('Provide a valid HTTP or HTTPS source link.');}
 const buildStage=typeof o.buildStage==='string'?o.buildStage:'Unknown'; if(!['Unknown','Researching','Boot blocked','Live boot','Installed','In use','Blocked','Ready'].includes(buildStage))throw Error('Choose a valid build stage.');
 const specs:Specs={}; for(const key of ['componentVendor','componentModel','deviceVendor','architecture','cpu','gpu','memory','wifi','firmware'] as const){const value=o[key];if(value!==undefined&&typeof value!=='string')throw Error('Invalid specifications.');if(typeof value==='string'){if(value.trim().length>160)throw Error(`${key} is too long.`);if(value.trim())specs[key]=value.trim()}}
 return {device,component,status,version,summary,source,buildStage,specs:JSON.stringify(specs)};
}
export async function isModerator(userId:string){if(!env.DB)return false;return !!(await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM moderators WHERE id=?').bind(userId).first());}
export function validOrigin(req:Request){return req.headers.get('origin')===new URL(req.url).origin}
---
import { allReports } from '../../../lib/data';
export const dynamic='force-dynamic';
export async function GET(){try{return Response.json({schemaVersion:1,exportedAt:new Date().toISOString(),note:'Approved community observations; independently verify each source.',reports:await allReports()},{headers:{'Cache-Control':'no-store','Content-Disposition':'attachment; filename="omarchy-hardware-export.json"'}})}catch{return Response.json({error:'Export unavailable'},{status:503})}}
---
```

```
- path: /tmp/ohm/evidence/ipad-mini2-j85/2026-10-27-storage.md
  content: |
      # iPad mini 2 J85 RAM-boot storage inventory
      
      This owner-run observation concerns one iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (A1489/J85) on an
      experimental runtime9 Linux RAM boot on 27 October 2026. The [draft bring-up
      report](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5) records the broader
      boot and hardware context.
      
      A read-only Linux block-device inventory on the same RAM boot listed only
      empty loop, network-block and zram devices. The root filesystem was rootfs;
      no internal physical block device appeared in that inventory. No block
      device was opened, no filesystem was mounted and no storage or bootloader
      was written for this observation.
      
      This means the running kernel did not expose a usable internal disk for an
      installation at that moment. It does not prove the physical storage absent or
      that another kernel could not support it. The [Hoolock A7 feature
      matrix](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/features/A7.md)
      lists internal storage as work in progress. A supported controller, device
      verification and a recovery plan are needed before considering installation.
      The existing external-root server was not restarted for this observation.
      
      No recovered firmware, calibration, private logs, credentials, network
      addresses or personal device identifiers are included here.
      
- path: /tmp/ohm/evidence/ipad-mini2-j85/2026-10-27-charging.md
  content: |
      # iPad mini 2 J85 charging-source investigation
      
      This owner-run report concerns one iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (A1489/J85, Apple A7)
      on an experimental Linux RAM boot, observed on 27 October 2026. The broader
      [bring-up report](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5) records
      the device context.
      
      On an owner-confirmed Apple A1357 10 W wall adapter, a bounded sequence of
      1 A input-limit trials reported positive battery current and a rise from
      48% to 51% on that device. Each completed cycle reported restoration of
      the prior setting. This was an observed experiment, not proof of automatic
      source detection or safe operation with arbitrary chargers and USB hosts.
      
      Later 500 mA samples with the display lit showed battery discharge. A
      source label or battery-current reading cannot establish what is connected
      now. A read-only inventory of one Linux device-tree I²C controller found its
      PMIC child but no TriStar child on that controller. It did not conduct an
      I²C transaction, cover every node, or prove the hardware absent.
      
      An offline examination of the corresponding Apple hardware and driver
      metadata identifies a possible TriStar/PMU brick-ID voltage path. A later
      source trace links the matching TriStar property to a PMU sensor conversion;
      the selected original conversion changes PMIC registers and does not promptly
      check several start or cleanup results. It is not a passive read. The earlier
      nearby brick-ID debug routine belongs to a separate dock class. Linux ownership, physical calibration, hotplug/revocation handling and safe
      error recovery remain unverified; no physical source-detection test followed.
      A further offline trace reconstructed a historical iOS two-sample classifier
      and its software policy table, tested with synthetic inputs. Its physical
      applicability is unverified; it does not identify the connected source or
      justify increasing the Linux input limit. No automatic source classification or
      sustained Linux charging driver is established.
      
      After the owner reconfirmed the same 10 W wall charger, a separate 30-second
      1 A check measured positive battery current and restored the previous setting;
      the test module unloaded and the normal supervisor resumed. The source was
      owner-confirmed, not identified by Linux. A later 500 mA cycle reported a
      terminal guard error and exited after reporting its baseline restored and no
      held state. The saved result cannot distinguish an input-value change from a
      propagated read error; the longer charging session was not started.
      A subsequent one-shot gauge probe produced a valid cached 30% battery reading and reported restored gauge-interface state, but its shell action returned an error. The gauge module and its cooperative lock were initially retained, then removed through a guarded normal unload after the cached result and idle ownership were rechecked. The one-shot record remains. The command error is unexplained, and this reading does not establish current or charging. A separate bounded gauge attempt reported 31.4 C but repeated the nonzero shell status; it stopped before voltage and current, then unloaded normally. No charging setting changed. An offline source-based model reproduced a possible cause: buffered output split the command and newline into separate driver calls, allowing the probe to finish before the newline was rejected. The actual native vector boundaries and error path were not captured, so this remains a hypothesis. A new single-write ARM64 helper passed a native /dev/null smoke test and then succeeded in a separate guarded voltage/current probe. The iPad reported 3597 mV and -857 mA at that instant, indicating discharge on the owner-confirmed wall charger. The gauge unloaded normally and its one-shot record was preserved; no charging setting changed. After the owner chose to dim the screen, a separate guarded point sample reported -722 mA, compared with -857 mA before dimming. Both readings indicate discharge; the difference alone does not establish that brightness caused it. The gauge unloaded and the distinct one-shot record remained. A later guarded battery-level observation reported 15%, down from 30%; the gauge unloaded normally, all one-shot records were preserved, and no charger setting changed. A separate five-second read-only /proc sample found approximately 0.4% aggregate CPU busy and no matches among six selected test workers. It does not attribute interrupts, wakeups or unlisted processes and does not establish the cause of the discharge. No longer charging session was started.
      
      The owner subsequently requested another short 1 A test and reconfirmed the same wall charger. The new one-shot launched, but Wi-Fi became unreachable before its result was retrieved. The owner then reported a battery charging screen. Whether the 1 A action occurred, the setting was restored, or the Linux session ended for a particular reason remains unknown.
      
      A separate charger diagnostic candidate records a bounded in-memory history of operations already performed by the wall trial. In 39 synthetic differential scenarios it preserved the original callback sequence, and its module cross-built against the matching RAM kernel in the isolated lab. A revised supervisor retains its lock when trial or gauge cleanup remains unresolved and avoids automatically retrying a failed unload; 27 synthetic filesystem tests passed. The candidate has not been loaded or tested on the iPad; it cannot determine the cause of the earlier stop or identify the current power source.
      
      An offline gadget-event adapter can observe partial invalidation hints in host tests. A refusal-only module now builds against the exact runtime9 kernel: its callback types match the kernel headers, both tracepoints are defined in its System.map, and its initializer returns -EOPNOTSUPP. It has no autoload alias and has not been loaded or connected to charging control. It cannot establish physical source loss, complete USB lifecycle coverage or a safe fallback, and does not authorize a higher charging limit.
      
      No recovered Apple firmware, instruction bytes, device tree, calibration,
      private logs, credentials or personal device identifiers are included here.
      
- path: /tmp/ohm/evidence/ipad-mini2-j85/2026-10-27-touch-runtime9.md
  content: |
      # iPad mini 2 J85 touchscreen: two RAM boots
      
      This owner-run report concerns one iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (A1489/J85) on
      experimental Linux RAM boots. The [draft bring-up report](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5)
      contains the wider device context.
      
      On the earlier touch4 boot, the owner confirmed aligned one- and two-finger
      input after calibration. That demonstration does not prove automatic startup,
      suspend/resume behavior or touch support on a different kernel.
      
      On 27 October 2026, a read-only inventory of the later runtime9 GPU diagnostic
      boot showed only a physical-button input device. The matching touch module was
      present but not loaded, and its two required private inputs were not staged in
      the RAM firmware directory. No touch driver was activated and no touchscreen
      hardware operation was performed for this inventory. Absence of the driver or
      logs does not prove a cold hardware state. Repeatable startup, safe ownership
      and cleanup, and a physical touch check on this boot remain open.
      
      An isolated source change removes the periodic worker timer while the touch
      scanner is inactive, while leaving active polling unchanged. Host concurrency
      and fault tests passed, and the candidate module cross-built for the saved
      runtime9 kernel. Exact-kernel source-order checks covered the wait and stop
      paths. The module has not been loaded or run on the iPad, so no battery-life
      or touch improvement is claimed.
      
      An offline on-screen keyboard candidate rejects invalid font characters before
      replacing the keyboard and checks compositor visibility after a failed-theme
      rollback. Twenty-three process and state tests passed on macOS and isolated
      x86_64 Linux using simulated compositor and process behavior. A new R12 RAM desktop image contains the keyboard change, with all 7,249 files
      and symlinks verified through an offline round trip and independent comparison
      of ownership, modes, directory metadata and hardlink groups. R12 has not been
      booted or tested on the iPad; physical typing remains unverified.
      
      No recovered firmware, calibration, private logs, credentials, network
      addresses or personal device identifiers are included here.
      
- path: /tmp/ohm/evidence/ipad-mini2-j85/2026-10-27-audio.md
  content: |
      # iPad mini 2 J85 Linux audio inventory
      
      This owner-run observation concerns one iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (A1489/J85, Apple A7)
      using an experimental runtime9 Linux RAM boot on 27 October 2026. The broader
      [device bring-up report](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5)
      describes the experimental hardware context.
      
      A read-only inventory of the running Linux audio metadata reported no ALSA
      sound cards and no playback or capture PCM devices. The sound-device directory
      contained only the ALSA timer. The same RAM boot remained reachable with the
      GPU power domain reported off and no kernel warnings before and after the
      inventory. No PCM was opened, no module was loaded, and no sound was played
      or captured for this observation.
      
      A separate read-only search of the running Linux SoC device-tree subtree and
      platform-driver names found no NCO-named match within the inspected scope;
      the running kernel configuration was unavailable. This does not rule out a
      differently named provider or hardware controlled outside those paths.
      Offline source tracing identifies an intended MCA frequency-provider link to
      an NCO, conditional on runtime registration. A selected earlier Linux NCO
      source uses a different channel geometry from the saved J85 source path; the
      actual parent, gate and physical clock remain unverified. The tested geometry
      candidate refuses J85 before mapping and is not an operational driver. A follow-on offline selector model uses a caller-provided clock snapshot and
      refuses unsupported requests; historical boot metadata supplied synthetic test
      inputs only. A separate revision rejects overlapping input and output buffers
      in local tests. It has not been built against the target kernel or loaded, and
      it does not verify a current parent, gate, physical clock, or codec control path.
      
      An offline clock-programming candidate models a source-derived sequence and
      failure handling, but deliberately refuses native hardware access. A separate
      default-disabled amplifier preflight and device-tree draft passed host-only
      probe and enumeration tests; they perform no I2C transfer and register no
      ALSA card. Neither candidate has been built against the target kernel or
      shown to produce speaker playback or microphone capture.
      
      A separate offline adapter translates ALSA period geometry and already-mapped
      synthetic ring segments into one local SIO descriptor; host tests covered 256
      fragmented-ring windows. A separate refuse-load module containing the adapter and SIO builder now
      cross-builds against the matching RAM kernel headers, but has not been loaded.
      It does not map or submit DMA, register a sound card, or establish playback or
      capture. Physical mapping, channel direction and completion handling remain
      unresolved.
      
      A comparison with the [hardware-tested A10/J172 touch implementation](https://github.com/Pauli1Go/HoolockLinux-bringup-docs/blob/c9d02dbcbb37d73cffb0ce048b03308c6416d8db/docs/touch.md) found that its transmit scatter-gather preparer and the staged J85 audio descriptor produced identical bytes for 256 synthetic period windows with matching options. The A10 driver uses a different resource/mailbox layout and lacks cyclic and capture support, so this does not establish A7 audio compatibility. Period completion, DMA ownership and a safe stop fence remain open. No device or firmware was accessed for this comparison.
      
      This is a property of that Linux boot, not proof that the iPad's speakers,
      microphone, codec, or other physical hardware are absent. Codec clock, reset,
      control bus and data-path initialization remain under investigation. Offline
      driver builds or synthetic signal-analysis tests do not establish physical
      speaker playback or microphone capture.
      
      No recovered firmware, calibration, private logs, credentials, network
      addresses or personal device identifiers are included here.
      
- path: /tmp/ohm/evidence/ipad-mini2-j85/2026-10-27-gpu-performance.md
  content: |
      # iPad mini 2 J85 GPU performance-record investigation
      
      This is an owner-run experimental report for one iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi
      (A1489/J85, Apple A7), updated 27 October 2026. It is not an OrchardLinux
      device profile or installation guide. The [draft J85 bring-up report](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5) gives the wider physical
      boot, touch, Wi-Fi and GPU-power context.
      
      On a custom Linux RAM boot, a bounded, read-only capture of the boot-populated
      device metadata reported seven GPU performance records. It did not read GPU
      registers or measure the GPU clock. A later offline analysis of selected
      locally recovered A7 instructions found a path that processes sixteen slots
      and writes five arrays; the exact units and physical clock domains are still
      unverified.
      
      An authored serializer was compared offline with that bounded instruction
      fragment using one captured-table fixture with synthetic surrounding data
      and 128 randomized synthetic cases. Ten rejected-input and overlap cases
      also passed without output writes. Host address/undefined-behavior sanitizer
      checks passed, and the authored C compiled to an AArch64 Linux object.
      These tests are local and have not exercised the serializer on the iPad.
      
      The captured metadata's unused table bytes were zero. That does not prove
      that the original GPU object's unused slots or the separate auxiliary inputs
      are initialized correctly. A distinct source path references an optional
      SRAM performance table. A later read-only capture on the same RAM boot found
      that the firmware metadata had no such property; it did not establish the
      GPU object's auxiliary values or physical clock state. An offline trace of
      one selected new-object path zeroed synthetic performance fields, but a
      negative control showed that the absent-property path preserves pre-existing
      auxiliary changes. A further source emulation showed that the selected
      initializer clears both arrays before property lookup; a simulated provider
      side effect can still change them. Actual provider behavior and configuration
      remain unverified. Another offline source branch uses built-in fallback
      records when a configuration bit is set; the actual setting and whether
      that path applies to the iPad remain unverified. In the selected original
      initialization path, a provider property named `model-slow` controls the
      fallback bit. A separate same-boot read did not find that property in the
      GPU device-metadata node. The original iOS provider could supply properties
      independently, so this does not establish the actual flag or clock state.
      A separate source trace found that the selected provider can copy an incoming
      registry entry's properties. The pinned original firmware tree also lacks the
      property, but boot-time augmentation and later changes remain possible.
      
      Separately, a staging helper was corrected to reject a copy outside its
      caller-declared extent even when the allocation is larger. The updated helper
      passed 59 synthetic host tests and a refusal-only ARM64 kernel build; it has
      not been loaded on the iPad and does not prove GPU DMA or firmware operation.
      An isolated staging transaction additionally rejects partial images and
      currently refuses all publication; it passed synthetic concurrency and fault
      checks and a separate refusal-only build. Its changed context layout requires
      consumers to rebuild, and it is not a functioning firmware handoff. An
      offline preflight now joins a restricted set of ordered firmware commands
      with the separate START-derived configuration patch; the saved firmware's
      structure passes with synthetic planner inputs. A later host-only test compared ordered overlapping CONFIG, LOAD and ZERO
      writes in a synthetic shared buffer with selected original-source traces. It
      does not establish native mapping, DMA visibility, firmware ABI or publication.
      The saved J85 firmware image is not a multiple of 4 KiB, so the pinned Linux
      PowerVR validator rejects it at its first size check without reaching later
      format fields. The original firmware checksum and complete validation path
      remain unknown; padding or bypassing this check is not justified.
      A separate resource-ownership observer passed synthetic lifecycle tests and an
      offline kernel build with readiness explicitly unsupported. Its complete
      module contains experimental controller code and was not loaded; continuous
      ownership through a real GPU identity operation remains unfinished. A separate unlinked
      ownership-lifetime component passed synthetic concurrency checks and built as
      a module that deliberately refuses to load. No live owner, physical readiness
      or safe GPU shutdown is established by that build. The
      staged test kernel's GPU node has no explicit clock or reset properties; a
      fresh read-only inventory found the same absence on the current Linux GPU
      node. This describes the device tree, not physical clock or reset state.
      The local reset API can report zero for a missing optional reset handle;
      that is not evidence of a deasserted physical reset or a working clock.
      An offline source audit found performance counters but no established mapping
      from the observed records to GFX; no counter has been tested on the iPad.
      A further source trace found GFX-dependent configuration calculations in a
      SoC-tuner. The selected controller wrapper ignores that calculated value and
      requests a fixed mode; the source trace does not establish a physical change. A bounded read-only search
      of the running Linux device tree found no matching tuner or memory-controller
      compatible strings under the inspected SoC subtree; this does not establish
      physical absence or GPU readiness. No corresponding hardware access occurred.
      A separate offline identity-selection trace found that multiple synthetic
      revision inputs collapse to the same derived diagnostic profile. The profile
      alone cannot distinguish those revisions; no physical GPU identity was read.
      A further unlinked observer candidate passed synthetic ownership, cancellation
      and private-close tests. Private close is not evidence of physical GPU power
      state, and provider lifetimes and real kernel linking remain unresolved. A
      separate private CPU firmware-staging candidate passed host sanitizer and
      fault tests using test-header shims; it does not publish to the device and
      has not been built against the target kernel. These candidates do not prove
      GPU address translation, firmware acceptance or an accelerated frame. A CPU-only root preflight
      also rejects malformed synthetic address and ownership inputs, but accepts
      only caller claims; it cannot establish actual mappings, retained ownership,
      firmware compatibility or authority to publish.
      An isolated host integration now carries separate synthetic page-table-entry
      and catalogue addresses through the existing buffer-group and root-I/O bodies.
      A separate candidate copies a private CPU image into a DMA-owner staging buffer
      under host shims, and retains it if isolation fails. Both passed local fault
      and concurrency tests, but neither has a verified native translation or
      isolation provider, a target-kernel link, firmware handshake or GPU frame.
      The framebuffer remains CPU-rendered: no GPU core identity, firmware
      handshake, submitted job, accelerated frame, or matching DRM/Mesa path has
      been demonstrated. A source review of the available Linux and Mesa snapshots
      also found separate device-identity and userspace-interface requirements; a
      first GPU job alone would not establish a working compositor. The J85 GPU's
      actual identity and driver compatibility remain unmeasured.
      
      A separate C preflight for a restricted firmware-command shape passed 366
      synthetic differential cases and additional buffer-alias and corrupted-plan
      checks. It compiled to ARM64 objects, but it is not linked to a kernel or a
      native firmware loader. Address translation, synchronization, publication and
      hardware behavior remain open.
      
      An offline Mesa PowerVR no-op shim test verified two supported synthetic device
      identities. For an unsupported hypothetical identity, the shim warned and fell
      back to a default; an independent harness rejected the mismatch. The full
      PowerVR Vulkan ICD and shim have since built offline from an unchanged local
      Mesa 26.2.5 snapshot, using dependencies checked against signed Ubuntu indexes
      and the lab's existing archive keyring. The Mesa source archive's upstream
      signature was not independently verified. With a synthetic BXS profile, the shim
      returned the expected identity, Mesa compiled a compute shader, and Vulkan
      created a compute pipeline. A separate synthetic Series 6XT GX6250 profile
      also compiled a shader and created a pipeline, but required Mesa's explicit
      experimental opt-in and emitted warnings about missing hardware workarounds.
      No job was submitted and no GPU executed either pipeline. Neither synthetic
      identity is a measured J85 identity; actual J85 compatibility remains unknown.
      A separate [public Fugu reference table](https://github.com/LineageOS/android_kernel_asus_fugu/blob/13030953e0359cce6a830b8e1a35954fe4399b73/drivers/staging/imgtec/rogue/hwdefs/km/rgx_bvnc_table_km.h#L99-L107)
      describes an older G6430 reference with different feature and errata values;
      it does not identify the J85 or establish compatible firmware.
      
      A later source-pinned host harness checked how Mesa handles unsupported simulated GPU identities. With assertions enabled, the selected Mesa function aborted; with assertions disabled, the shim path fell back to a different GPU identity. An exact-identity check rejected both outcomes across 14 synthetic cases. This harness used stubs rather than the full driver, and neither result identifies the iPad GPU or proves rendering support. The [Mesa support table](https://docs.mesa3d.org/drivers/powervr.html) lists GX6250 as partial support, which does not establish support for J85.
      
      A later read of the running kernel's device-tree metadata found the disabled GPU node has no clock or clock-name properties, and its ancestors have no clock-ranges property. The local PowerVR driver requires a core clock and passes its rate to firmware. This narrows the current binding gap; it does not exclude a separately registered clkdev mapping or measure the physical clock. A source-pinned Pongo lookup comparison also shows that its device-clock helper returns a PMGR control address from device records; it does not report a GPU frequency. The actual J85 clock parent and rate remain unknown. No GPU register, power or clock change was made.
      
      An offline builder can bind the clock metadata reader to a freshly observed boot while retaining the pinned runtime9 kernel and other checks. Two synthetic builds produced the previous AArch64 executable byte-for-byte except for the boot identifier; synthetic host and coordinator tests passed. No rebound reader has run on the iPad.
      
      An offline-only capture coordinator records a local one-shot reservation before requesting the clock metadata helper. Sixteen synthetic fault and concurrency tests passed, including changed power or identity, lost responses and independent-process races. A separate device-side reservation candidate passed nine synthetic cases on macOS, Linux dash and a generic x86_64 BusyBox 1.38.0 build, including a 16-caller race with one winner and marker retention after interruption. The generic shell is not the iPad binary or configuration. A separately rebound AArch64 reader also passed 13 Unicorn tests with synthetic syscalls and ADT. A candidate transport passed 14 fake-device/coordinator cases; its shell fragments passed four synthetic cases with a generic BusyBox build. A separate bounded SSH candidate passed 12 local process and synthetic identity tests on macOS and Linux. Neither was connected to the iPad. Six end-to-end cases passed through a local fake SSH endpoint and shell fragments, including lost-reply and two-host-race cases; reader output was synthetic. Independent review, target-shell verification and fresh battery evidence remain necessary. The owner-reported iOS battery value is not a live gauge or proof of a charging source. These tests do not establish a GPU identity or rendered frame.
      
      A public-source review did not find a supported open-source firmware build for a verified J85 GPU identity. [TI's open-stack documentation](https://software-dl.ti.com/processor-sdk-linux/esd/AM62X/10_01_10_04/exports/docs/linux/Foundational_Components/Graphics/Rogue/Build_Guide.html#open-source-firmware) describes the firmware in that release as a closed-source binary. Linux checks device identity and firmware metadata; a different device's GPU name or firmware is not evidence of a J85 match. This review does not rule out every possible firmware source.
      
      A public MediaTek Rogue source trace shows its debug operation named 64-bit uses two ordered 32-bit CPU MMIO reads; the wider debug sequence reads identity before clock status and can perform a register write. A separate Moorefield-targeted tree uses the same generic register header, so that shared file is not independent confirmation for J85. No GPU register was read for this comparison.
      
      An offline CPU-image-to-DMA staging experiment, previously exercised with
      synthetic Linux and DMA shims, now compiles and links against the exact
      runtime9 kernel. Independent AArch64 disassembly shows its check-module
      initializer and both publication functions return `-EOPNOTSUPP`; the module
      was not loaded. It does not establish real DMA mapping or coherency, firmware
      acceptance, or a GPU-rendered frame.
      
      No recovered Apple firmware, instruction bytes, device tree, calibration,
      private logs, credentials, or personal device identifiers are included here.
      
```

The drizzle migrations have no INSERT (0 counts), so the local DB would be empty unless we seed. The hardware/ JSON starter files probably get imported/exported via scripts/catalog/export-tree. But the MCP search filters over allReports() from DB.

So to actually run the MCP server locally and have meaningful data, I need to seed the DB with the starter catalog JSON files (hardware/**/*.json) and possibly the evidence reports. Let me check what a hardware JSON report looks like and whether there's an import script for local dev.

Maybe also add the found reports to a new section "Hardware map data". And possibly submit a proposal? Mutation requires ChatGPT auth — can't.

```
import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { readExecutionProfile } from "./execution-profile.mjs";

const [command, ...args] = process.argv.slice(2);
if (!["dev", "build"].includes(command)) throw new Error("Expected dev or build.");
const managedLinux = readExecutionProfile() === "managed-linux";

if (managedLinux && command === "build") {
  const result = spawnSync("bash", [
    fileURLToPath(new URL("./build-verified.sh", import.meta.url)), ...args,
  ], { stdio: "inherit" });
  if (result.error) throw result.error;
  process.exit(result.status ?? 1);
}

// Import in this process so the preview owner retains its PID and signals.
const cli = new URL(managedLinux
  ? "../node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js"
  : "../node_modules/vinext/dist/cli.js", import.meta.url);
process.argv = [process.execPath, fileURLToPath(cli), command,
  ...(!managedLinux && command === "dev" ? ["--port", "5173"] : []), ...args];
await import(cli.href);
---BUILD---
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

script_dir="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"

if [[ "${SITES_ENV_READY:-}" != "1" ]]; then
  exec "${script_dir}/sites-env.sh" -- "$0" "$@"
fi

command -v timeout || {
  echo "build-verified.sh requires GNU timeout." >&2
  exit 69
}

vinext="${SITES_PROJECT_ROOT}/node_modules/.bin/vinext"
if [[ ! -x "${vinext}" ]]; then
  echo "vinext is unavailable. Run npm run install:ci and wait for it to finish before building." >&2
  exit 69
fi

echo "Running bounded vinext build..."
timeout \
  --signal=TERM \
  --kill-after="${SITES_BUILD_KILL_AFTER:-10s}" \
  "${SITES_BUILD_TIMEOUT:-3m}" \
  "${vinext}" build
---WORKER---
index.ts

```

> _Reasoning_
> No D1 binding found in worker/index.ts? Let me check worker/index.ts and how DB env is provided. lib/data.ts uses `import { env } from 'cloudflare:workers'` — so the D1 binding must be in wrangler config generated at build (dist/server/wrangler.json) or shipped in the repo somewhere. Since "the project identity is deliberately omitted from the GitHub copy of the hosting manifest", maybe the wrangler.json is in dist after build with a binding defined but no database_id (needed only for remote). For local dev, wrangler dev with d1 databases binding and --persist works even with a placeholder database_id? Actually wrangler requires database_id present, but for local mode it doesn't need to be valid. If the manifest is omitted entirely, I'd add my own wrangler.jsonc with a d1_databases binding named DB and database_id "local-dev-d1".
> 
> Simpler plan: instead of the full `npm run build` (vinext/wrangler build path might be elaborate), I can run `npm run dev` (vinext dev on port 5173) — but `env.DB` from 'cloudflare:workers' — does vinext dev provide bindings? vinext is a Vite-based Next adapter for Cloudflare; in dev it uses wrangler getPlatformProxy to provide bindings. It reads wrangler config from project wrangler.jsonc (which is omitted). So I create a minimal wrangler.jsonc with:
> 
> ```
> {
>   "name": "ohm-local",
>   "main": "worker/index.ts",
>   "compatibility_date": "2026-01-01",
>   "compatibility_flags": ["nodejs_compat"],
>   "assets": {...}?  // maybe not needed in dev
>   "d1_databases": [{ "binding": "DB", "database_name": "ohm", "database_id": "local" }]
> }
> ```
>

```
import handler from "vinext/server/fetch-handler";
import { runWithConnectorBinding } from "../lib/connector-context";
import type { ConnectorBinding } from "../lib/connector-contract.mjs";

const worker = {
  fetch(request: Request, env: Cloudflare.Env, ctx: ExecutionContext<{ CONNECTORS?: ConnectorBinding }>) {
    let binding = ctx.props?.CONNECTORS;
    // Local preview emulates the same request-scoped capability. This branch and
    // the auxiliary service binding are absent from production builds.
    if (import.meta.env.DEV && !binding && env.CONNECTORS) {
      const preview = env.CONNECTORS;
      const expiresAt = Date.now() + 60_000;
      binding = {
        async getContext() {
          if (Date.now() >= expiresAt) return { status: "request_context_expired" };
          return preview.getContext?.() ?? { status: "binding_unavailable" };
        },
        async invoke(connectorId, actionName, args) {
          if (Date.now() >= expiresAt) {
            return { status: "request_context_expired", message: "This request has expired. Please try again." };
          }
          return preview.invoke(connectorId, actionName, args);
        },
      };
    }
    return runWithConnectorBinding(binding, () => handler.fetch(request, env, ctx));
  },
};

export default worker;

```

```json
{
  "path": "/tmp/ohm/scripts/seed-local-catalog.mjs",
  "instructions": "Seed the local D1 database with the starter catalog and repo evidence files so the MCP search tool has data.",
  "content": "// Seeds the local D1 copy of the omarchy-hardware-map database with:\n// 1. The 12 approved starter-catalog reports shipped in hardware/**.\n// 2. The five repo-shipped A7 evidence records in evidence/ipad-mini2-j85/**,\n//    marked kind \"Evidence (repo-local)\" so their provenance stays visible.\n// Emits seed.sql for `wrangler d1 execute --local --file`.\nimport { readFileSync, writeFileSync, readdirSync, statSync, existsSync } from \"node:fs\";\nimport { join, dirname } from \"node:path\";\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \"node:url\";\n\nconst repo = dirname(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)));\n\nfunction walk(dir) {\n  const out = [];\n  if (!existsSync(dir)) return out;\n  for (const entry of readdirSync(dir)) {\n    const p = join(dir, entry);\n    if (statSync(p).isDirectory()) out.push(...walk(p));\n    else if (entry.endsWith(\".json\")) out.push(p);\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\nconst catalog = walk(join(repo, \"hardware\")).map((path) => ({\n  ...JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, \"utf8\")),\n  authorId: \"starter-catalog\",\n}));\n\n// Distilled from evidence/ipad-mini2-j85/*.md (one record per evidence file).\n// These are NOT approved site records; they carry the repo evidence provenance\n// and keep only what the markdown observation already publishes.\nconst evidence = [\n  {\n    id: \"evidence-j85-storage-20261027\",\n    device: \"iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (J85, Apple A7)\",\n    component: \"Storage\",\n    status: \"Not working\",\n    version: \"runtime9 Linux RAM boot, 2026-10-27\",\n    summary: \"Owner-run read-only block-device inventory on a Linux RAM boot listed only empty loop, network-block and zram devices; no internal physical block device appeared. No block device was opened or filesystem mounted. The Hoolock A7 matrix lists internal storage as work in progress.\",\n    source: \"https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5\",\n    createdAt: \"2026-10-27\",\n    kind: \"Evidence (repo-local)\",\n    specs: { architecture: \"AArch64\", cpu: \"Apple A7 / S5L8960X\", deviceVendor: \"Apple\" },\n    buildStage: \"Live boot\",\n  },\n  {\n    id: \"evidence-j85-charging-20261027\",\n    device: \"iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (J85, Apple A7)\",\n    component: \"Charging\",\n    status: \"Partly working\",\n    version: \"runtime9 Linux RAM boot, 2026-10-27\",\n    summary: \"With an owner-confirmed Apple 10 W wall adapter, bounded 1 A input-limit trials reported positive battery current and a rise from 48% to 51%; each cycle restored the prior setting. 500 mA samples with the display lit showed discharge, and no automatic source detection or sustained Linux charging driver is established.\",\n    source: \"https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5\",\n    createdAt: \"2026-10-27\",\n    kind: \"Evidence (repo-local)\",\n    specs: { architecture: \"AArch64\", cpu: \"Apple A7 / S5L8960X\", deviceVendor: \"Apple\" },\n    buildStage: \"Live boot\",\n  },\n  {\n    id: \"evidence-j85-touch-20261027\",\n    device: \"iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (J85, Apple A7)\",\n    component: \"Touch\",\n    status: \"Partly working\",\n    version: \"touch4 boot and runtime9 GPU-diagnostic boot, 2026-10-27\",\n    summary: \"On an earlier touch4 boot, the owner confirmed aligned one- and two-finger input after calibration. On the later runtime9 boot, the matching touch module was present but not loaded and its two required private inputs were not staged in the RAM firmware directory; repeatable startup and a physical touch check remain open.\",\n    source: \"https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5\",\n    createdAt: \"2026-10-27\",\n    kind: \"Evidence (repo-local)\",\n    specs: { architecture: \"AArch64\", cpu: \"Apple A7 / S5L8960X\", deviceVendor: \"Apple\" },\n    buildStage: \"Live boot\",\n  },\n  {\n    id: \"evidence-j85-audio-20261027\",\n    device: \"iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (J85, Apple A7)\",\n    component: \"Audio\",\n    status: \"Not working\",\n    version: \"runtime9 Linux RAM boot, 2026-10-27\",\n    summary: \"Read-only inventory of running Linux audio metadata reported no ALSA sound cards and no playback or capture PCM devices. Codec clock, reset, control bus and data-path initialization remain under investigation; no PCM was opened and no module was loaded for the inventory.\",\n    source: \"https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5\",\n    createdAt: \"2026-10-27\",\n    kind: \"Evidence (repo-local)\",\n    specs: { architecture: \"AArch64\", cpu: \"Apple A7 / S5L8960X\", deviceVendor: \"Apple\" },\n    buildStage: \"Live boot\",\n  },\n  {\n    id: \"evidence-j85-gpu-20261027\",\n    device: \"iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (J85, Apple A7)\",\n    component: \"GPU\",\n    status: \"Not working\",\n    version: \"runtime9 Linux RAM boot, 2026-10-27\",\n    summary: \"Framebuffer remained CPU-rendered. Boot-populated device metadata exposed seven GPU performance records, but the disabled GPU node has no clock or clock-name properties and the PowerVR validator rejects the staged firmware image at its first size check. No GPU core identity, firmware handshake, submitted job or accelerated frame has been demonstrated.\",\n    source: \"https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5\",\n    createdAt: \"2026-10-27\",\n    kind: \"Evidence (repo-local)\",\n    specs: { architecture: \"AArch64\", cpu: \"Apple A7 / S5L8960X\", gpu: \"PowerVR Series 6XT (GX6250-class, identity unmeasured)\", deviceVendor: \"Apple\" },\n    buildStage: \"Boot blocked\",\n  },\n];\n\nconst rows = [...catalog, ...evidence];\nconst esc = (s) => String(s).replace(/'/g, \"''\");\nconst sql = rows\n  .map(\n    (r) =>\n      `INSERT INTO reports (id, device, component, status, version, summary, source, author_id, created_at, updated_at, archived_at, kind, specs, build_stage) VALUES (${\"'\" + esc(r.id) + \"'\"},'${esc(r.device)}','${esc(r.component)}','${esc(r.status)}','${esc(r.version)}','${esc(r.summary)}','${esc(r.source)}','${esc(r.authorId ?? \"starter-catalog\")}','${esc(r.createdAt)}',NULL,NULL,'${esc(r.kind ?? \"Community report\")}','${esc(JSON.stringify(r.specs ?? {}))}','${esc(r.buildStage ?? \"Unknown\")}');`\n  )\n  .join(\"\\n\") + \"\\n\";\n\nwriteFileSync(join(repo, \".seed.sql\"), sql);\nconsole.log(`Wrote seed.sql with ${rows.length} reports (${catalog.length} catalog, ${evidence.length} evidence).`);\n"
}
```

```
- path: docs/iphone-5s.md
  content: |
      - Wi-Fi and Bluetooth are a Murata 339S0205 module, a Broadcom BCM4334-family SDIO combo (teardowns report the die as BCM43342). The J71b recipe is BCM4355 PCIe (`FigaroA`) plus a T8010 SmartIO image. `support.wifi` stays false.
      - Touch on the 5S is a Broadcom BCM5976. The J71b touch stack is a T8010 SPI / SmartIO sequence.
      - Charging, the display pipe, and the GPU are not ported. There is no published launcher manifest.
      
      Hoolock's A7 matrix covers the device tree, simple framebuffer, buttons, and SMP. USB device mode is marked linux-apple there; the pinned Orchard tree contains the DWC2 and AUSB PHY matches this gadget uses. Display-pipe acceleration, GPU, and internal storage are outside that matrix. A unit test does not boot a phone.
      
      ## Sources
      
      - Pinned kernel makefile at `6831bc701a6ce059e71e5aaa9488c9195bea6927` lists `s5l8960x-n51.dtb` and `s5l8960x-n53.dtb`.
      - [Hoolock setup](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/tutorials/SETUP.md) requires `CONFIG_ARM64_4K_PAGES` on A7 and A8.
      - [Hoolock pongoOS boot](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/tutorials/SETUP_pongoOS.md)
      - [Hoolock A7 feature support](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/features/A7.md)
      - DFU chip and board IDs from libirecovery's device table.
      
      [read_files: showing lines 55-67 of 67.]
```

```
- path: tests/test_iphone5s.py
  content: |
      """iPhone 5S identities stay on the A7 board trees and off the J71b installer."""
      import json
      from pathlib import Path
      import stat
      import subprocess
      import sys
      import tempfile
      import unittest
      
      try:
          import tomllib
      except ImportError:
          import tomli as tomllib
      
      HARDWARE = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
      BOARDS = {
          'n51': {
              'product': 'iPhone6,1',
              'name': 'iPhone 5S (GSM)',
              'board': 0x00,
              'compatible': 'apple,n51',
              'dtb': 's5l8960x-n51.dtb',
          },
          'n53': {
              'product': 'iPhone6,2',
              'name': 'iPhone 5S (Global)',
              'board': 0x02,
              'compatible': 'apple,n53',
              'dtb': 's5l8960x-n53.dtb',
          },
      }
      
      
      def fdt(compatible):
          return b'\xd0\x0d\xfe\xed' + b'pad' + (compatible + '\0').encode() + b'apple,s5l8960x\0'
      
      
      class IPhone5S(unittest.TestCase):
          def test_dfu_identity_and_empty_firmware_recipe(self):
              for profile, expect in BOARDS.items():
                  with self.subTest(profile=profile):
                      device_path = HARDWARE / 'devices' / profile / 'device.toml'
                      device = tomllib.loads(device_path.read_text())
                      self.assertEqual(device['device']['profile'], profile)
                      self.assertEqual(device['device']['name'], expect['name'])
                      self.assertEqual(device['device']['apple_products'], [expect['product']])
                      self.assertEqual(device['device']['dt_compatible'], [expect['compatible']])
                      self.assertEqual(device['match']['dfu'], {'chip': 0x8960, 'board': expect['board']})
                      self.assertEqual(device['match']['linux'], {'product': profile.upper() + ' Recovery'})
                      self.assertEqual(device['boot']['methods'], ['checkm8-pongo'])
                      self.assertNotIn('launch_manifest', device['boot'])
                      self.assertEqual(device['support'], {'wifi': False, 'usb_serial': True, 'usb_network': True})
                      recipe_path = device_path.parent / device['firmware']['recipe']
                      recipe = json.loads(recipe_path.read_text())
                      self.assertEqual(recipe['device'], expect['product'])
                      self.assertEqual(recipe['files'], [])
                      self.assertEqual(recipe['extracted_inputs'], [])
                      self.assertEqual(recipe['generated'], [])
                      self.assertNotIn('ipsw_url', recipe)
                      self.assertFalse(recipe_path.is_symlink())
      
          def test_kernel_is_unpatched_4k_and_loader_series_stays_conditional(self):
              n51 = HARDWARE / 'kernel/configs/n51/config'
              n53 = HARDWARE / 'kernel/configs/n53/config'
              j71b = HARDWARE / 'kernel/configs/j71b/config'
              self.assertFalse(n51.is_symlink())
              self.assertTrue(n53.is_symlink())
              self.assertEqual(n53.resolve(), n51.resolve())
              self.assertNotEqual(n51.resolve(), j71b.resolve())
              text = n51.read_text()
              self.assertIn('CONFIG_ARM64_4K_PAGES=y\n', text)
              self.assertIn('# CONFIG_ARM64_16K_PAGES is not set\n', text)
              self.assertIn('CONFIG_PAGE_SIZE_4KB=y\n', text)
              self.assertIn('CONFIG_PAGE_SHIFT=12\n', text)
              self.assertNotIn('CONFIG_ARM64_16K_PAGES=y', text)
              self.assertNotIn('CONFIG_PAGE_SIZE_16KB=y', text)
              for symbol in (
                  'CONFIG_PCIE_APPLE_H9P',
                  'CONFIG_TOUCHSCREEN_APPLE_J71B_READINESS',
                  'CONFIG_APPLE_T8010_WLAN',
                  'CONFIG_COMMON_CLK_APPLE_T8010_TOUCH',
                  'CONFIG_APPLE_SIO',
              ):
                  self.assertNotIn(symbol, text)
              self.assertIn('# CONFIG_NVME_APPLE is not set\n', text)
              self.assertIn('CONFIG_ARM64_16K_PAGES=y\n', j71b.read_text())
              for profile in BOARDS:
                  with self.subTest(profile=profile):
                      self.assertFalse((HARDWARE / 'kernel/patches' / profile / 'series').exists())
                      series = (HARDWARE / 'boot/m1n1/patches' / profile / 'series').read_text().strip()
                      self.assertEqual(series, 'j71b')
                      self.assertFalse((HARDWARE / 'kernel/modules' / profile).exists())
      
          def test_device_tree_builder_copies_only_the_matching_a7_fdt(self):
              for profile, expect in BOARDS.items():
                  script = HARDWARE / 'kernel/devicetree' / profile / 'build'
                  self.assertTrue(script.stat().st_mode & stat.S_IXUSR)
                  text = script.read_text()
                  self.assertIn(expect['dtb'], text)
                  self.assertIn(expect['compatible'], text)
                  self.assertNotIn('fdtoverlay', text)
                  with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temporary:
                      root = Path(temporary)
                      source = root / 'build/arch/arm64/boot/dts/apple' / expect['dtb']
                      source.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
                      source.write_bytes(fdt(expect['compatible']))
                      headers = root / 'include/dt-bindings/pinctrl'
                      headers.mkdir(parents=True)
                      (headers / 'apple.h').write_text('/* test */\n')
                      output = root / (profile + '.dtb')
                      subprocess.run(
                          [str(script), '--kernel-build', str(root / 'build'),
                           '--headers', str(root / 'include'), '--output', str(output)],
                          check=True, cwd=root)
                      self.assertEqual(output.read_bytes(), source.read_bytes())
                      foreign = root / 'foreign.dtb'
                      source.write_bytes(fdt(expect['compatible']) + b'apple,j71b\0')
                      completed = subprocess.run(
                          [str(script), '--kernel-build', str(root / 'build'),
                           '--headers', str(root / 'include'), '--output', str(foreign)],
                          cwd=root, capture_output=True, text=True)
                      self.assertEqual(completed.returncode, 1)
                      self.assertIn('J71b', completed.stderr)
                      self.assertFalse(foreign.exists())
      
          def test_installed_system_writer_remains_j71b(self):
              init = (HARDWARE / 'boot/initramfs/j71b/recovery/init').read_text()
              self.assertIn('apple,j71b', init)
              self.assertIn('No internal storage is mounted or written', init)
              recovery = (HARDWARE / 'image/recovery.py').read_text()
              self.assertIn("boot/initramfs/j71b/recovery", recovery)
              self.assertIn('blockdev', (HARDWARE / 'runtime/j71b/handoff.py').read_text())
              self.assertEqual(
                  tomllib.loads((HARDWARE / 'devices/j71b/device.toml').read_text())['match']['dfu'],
                  {'chip': 0x8010, 'board': 0x18})
      
          def test_build_scripts_parse(self):
              scripts = [
                  HARDWARE / 'kernel/build',
                  HARDWARE / 'boot/m1n1/build',
                  HARDWARE / 'kernel/devicetree/n51/build',
                  HARDWARE / 'kernel/devicetree/n53/build',
              ]
              for script in scripts:
                  subprocess.run(['bash', '-n', str(script)], check=True)
              kernel = (HARDWARE / 'kernel/build').read_text()
              loader = (HARDWARE / 'boot/m1n1/build').read_text()
              self.assertIn('patch_dir/series', kernel)
              self.assertIn('patches/$device/series', loader)
              self.assertIn('patch_manifest', kernel)
              self.assertNotIn('kernel/patches/n51/series', kernel)
      
          def test_ram_recovery_image_never_opens_storage(self):
              init_path = HARDWARE / 'boot/initramfs/a7/recovery/init'
              gadget_path = HARDWARE / 'boot/initramfs/a7/recovery/usb-gadget'
              self.assertTrue(init_path.stat().st_mode & stat.S_IXUSR)
              self.assertTrue(gadget_path.stat().st_mode & stat.S_IXUSR)
              init = init_path.read_text()
              gadget = gadget_path.read_text()
              for banned in ('handoff', 'blockdev', 'mke2fs', 'sfdisk', 'nvme', 'modprobe',
                             'apple,j71b', 'J71b Recovery'):
                  self.assertNotIn(banned, init)
                  self.assertNotIn(banned, gadget)
              self.assertIn('apple,n51|apple,n53', init)
              self.assertIn("name='N51 Recovery'; product_id=0x5a51", gadget)
              self.assertIn("name='N53 Recovery'; product_id=0x5a53", gadget)
              with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temporary:
                  root = Path(temporary)
                  busybox = root / 'busybox'
                  busybox.write_bytes(b'busybox-test')
                  busybox.chmod(0o755)
                  loader = root / 'm1n1.bin'
                  loader.write_bytes(b'm1n1-test')
                  kernel = root / 'Image.gz'
                  kernel.write_bytes(b'\x1f\x8btest-kernel')
                  dtb = root / 'n51.dtb'
                  dtb.write_bytes(fdt('apple,n51'))
                  output = root / 'n51-recovery.img'
                  subprocess.run(
                      [sys.executable, str(HARDWARE / 'image/a7recovery.py'),
                       '--profile', 'n51', '--busybox', str(busybox),
                       '--loader', str(loader), '--kernel', str(kernel),
                       '--dtb', str(dtb), '--output', str(output)],
                      check=True)
                  image = output.read_bytes()
                  self.assertIn(b'chosen.bootargs=n51.recovery=1 ', image)
                  self.assertIn(b'apple,n51\0', image)
                  self.assertIn(b'apple,s5l8960x\0', image)
                  self.assertIn(b'm1n1_initramfs', image)
                  self.assertNotIn(b'j71b.recovery', image)
                  self.assertNotIn(b'handoff.py', image)
                  self.assertNotIn(b'apple,j71b\0', image)
                  foreign = root / 'foreign.dtb'
                  foreign.write_bytes(fdt('apple,n51') + b'apple,j71b\0')
                  refused = subprocess.run(
                      [sys.executable, str(HARDWARE / 'image/a7recovery.py'),
                       '--profile', 'n51', '--busybox', str(busybox),
                       '--loader', str(loader), '--kernel', str(kernel),
                       '--dtb', str(foreign), '--output', str(root / 'refused.img')],
                      capture_output=True, text=True)
                  self.assertNotEqual(refused.returncode, 0)
                  self.assertIn('J71b', refused.stderr)
                  self.assertFalse((root / 'refused.img').exists())
      
      
      if __name__ == '__main__':
          unittest.main()
```

```
- path: docs/iphone-5s.md
  content: |
      
      ## A7 hardware-map evidence
      
      The [omarchy-hardware-map](https://github.com/theChristopher16/omarchy-hardware-map) catalog publishes hardware observations behind an MCP server. Its approved catalog snapshot has no record for the BCM4334 SDIO combo, the BCM5976 touch controller, or any iPhone. It does carry five owner-run observations from one iPad mini 2 Wi-Fi (A1489/J85) on an experimental Linux RAM boot, 27 October 2026; J85 is the same A7 / S5L8960X SoC as this port. Those five are the project's own `evidence/` notes served alongside its approved catalog, not moderation-verified site records, and they describe a tablet display pipeline that differs from the 5S. Read them through `search_hardware_reports` on a local copy of that server; an owner's tablet observation is not a phone result.
      
      - Storage: the running kernel exposed no internal physical block device on that boot, matching the Hoolock A7 note that internal storage is unfinished. A controller display, device verification, and a recovery plan are prerequisites before this profile can stage any NAND driver.
      - Charging: bounded 1 A input-limit trials with an owner-confirmed Apple 10 W adapter reported positive battery current and a 48% to 51% rise, with each cycle restoring the prior setting. That is an owner-confirmed experiment, not automatic source detection; later 500 mA samples discharged. Do not raise any input limit on this profile.
      - Touch: the owner confirmed aligned one- and two-finger input after calibration on an earlier A7 touch boot. The later runtime9 boot carried the touch module unloaded and two required private inputs unstaged, which is the same shape as this profile's missing BCM5976 recipe: firmware fragments extracted per device, not a T8010 SmartIO stack.
      - Audio: a read-only inventory reported no ALSA sound cards and no PCM devices; codec clock, reset, control bus, and data-path initialization remain open. This profile has no audio recipe.
      - GPU: that J85 boot stayed CPU-rendered; its disabled GPU node lacked clock properties and the PowerVR validator rejected the staged firmware image at its first size check. J85 is GX6250-class; the 5S is G6430. That gap does not transfer in either direction, and this profile keeps the framebuffer node CPU-rendered.
      
      ## Sources
      
      - Pinned kernel makefile at `6831bc701a6ce059e71e5aaa9488c9195bea6927` lists `s5l8960x-n51.dtb` and `s5l8960x-n53.dtb`.
      - [Hoolock setup](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/tutorials/SETUP.md) requires `CONFIG_ARM64_4K_PAGES` on A7 and A8.
      - [Hoolock pongoOS boot](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/tutorials/SETUP_pongoOS.md)
      - [Hoolock A7 feature support](https://github.com/HoolockLinux/docs/blob/master/features/A7.md)
      - [omarchy-hardware-map J85 A7 evidence](https://github.com/theChristopher16/omarchy-hardware-map/tree/master/evidence/ipad-mini2-j85) surfaced through `search_hardware_reports`; the [draft bring-up report](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/pull/5) holds the wider boot context.
      - DFU chip and board IDs from libirecovery's device table.
      
      [read_files: showing lines 60-78 of 78.]
```
