````
- path: kernel/build
  content: |
      #!/usr/bin/env bash
      set -euo pipefail
      
      here=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
      device=${1:-}
      [[ $# -gt 0 ]] && shift
      dest=
      resume=false
      while (($#)); do
        case "$1" in
          --output) [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || { echo 'Missing --output value' >&2; exit 2; }; dest=$2; shift 2;;
          --resume) resume=true; shift;;
          *) echo "Unknown argument: $1" >&2; exit 2;;
        esac
      done
      if [[ ! "$device" =~ ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$ || -z "$dest" || -e "$dest" ]]; then
        echo 'Usage: ./kernel/build DEVICE --output DIRECTORY [--resume]' >&2
        exit 2
      fi
      config="$here/configs/$device/config"
      patch_dir="$here/patches/$device"
      if [[ -f "$patch_dir/series" ]]; then
        series=$(tr -d '[:space:]' < "$patch_dir/series")
        [[ "$series" =~ ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$ && "$series" != "$device" && -d "$here/patches/$series" ]] || {
          echo "Invalid kernel patch series for $device" >&2; exit 1;
        }
        patch_dir="$here/patches/$series"
      fi
      patches=()
      for patch in "$patch_dir/"*.patch; do
        [[ -f "$patch" ]] && patches+=("$patch")
      done
      patch_manifest() {
        [[ ${#patches[@]} -eq 0 ]] && return 0
        local patch
        for patch in "${patches[@]}"; do
          printf '%s %s\n' "$(git hash-object "$patch")" "${patch##*/}"
        done
      }
      dtb_builder="$here/devicetree/$device/build"
      [[ -f "$config" && -x "$dtb_builder" ]] || {
        echo "Missing kernel config or device-tree builder for $device" >&2; exit 1;
      }
      command -v git >/dev/null || { echo 'Git is required' >&2; exit 1; }
      if [[ ${ORCHARD_BUILD_NATIVE:-0} != 1 ]]; then
        podman info >/dev/null || { echo 'Podman is unavailable' >&2; exit 1; }
      fi
      url=$(awk -F= '$1=="url" {print substr($0,5)}' "$here/upstream.lock")
      commit=$(awk -F= '$1=="commit" {print $2}' "$here/upstream.lock")
      [[ "$url" == https://github.com/OrchardLinux/linux.git && "$commit" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ ]] || {
        echo 'Unexpected kernel/upstream.lock' >&2; exit 1;
      }
      work="$here/.work/$device"
      source="$work/source"
      build_output="$work/output"
      if "$resume"; then
        [[ -d "$source" && -d "$build_output" ]] || { echo "No build to resume: $work" >&2; exit 1; }
        if [[ ! -d "$source/.git" ]]; then git -C "$source" init -q; fi
        remote=$(git -C "$source" remote get-url origin 2>/dev/null || true)
        if [[ -z "$remote" ]]; then
          git -C "$source" remote add origin "$url"
        elif [[ "$remote" != "$url" ]]; then
          echo "Unexpected kernel remote: $remote" >&2; exit 1
        fi
        head=$(git -C "$source" rev-parse --verify HEAD 2>/dev/null || true)
        if [[ -z "$head" ]]; then
          echo "Fetching kernel source at $commit (this may take a while)..."
          git -C "$source" fetch --progress --depth 1 origin "$commit"
          git -C "$source" checkout -q --detach FETCH_HEAD
        elif [[ "$head" != "$commit" ]]; then
          echo 'Wrong kernel revision' >&2; exit 1
        fi
        echo 'Checking kernel patches...'
        applied="$work/applied-patches"
        if [[ -f "$applied" ]]; then
          cmp "$applied" <(patch_manifest) || { echo 'Kernel patch set changed; use a fresh build directory' >&2; exit 1; }
        else
          if [[ ${#patches[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then
            for patch in "${patches[@]}"; do
              if git -C "$source" apply --reverse --check "$patch" 2>/dev/null; then continue; fi
              git -C "$source" apply --check "$patch"
              git -C "$source" apply "$patch"
            done
          fi
          patch_manifest > "$applied"
        fi
      else
        [[ ! -e "$work" ]] || { echo "Build directory exists: $work (use --resume)" >&2; exit 1; }
        mkdir -p "$source" "$build_output"
        git -C "$source" init -q
        git -C "$source" remote add origin "$url"
        echo "Fetching kernel source at $commit (this may take a while)..."
        git -C "$source" fetch --progress --depth 1 origin "$commit"
        git -C "$source" checkout -q --detach FETCH_HEAD
        [[ $(git -C "$source" rev-parse HEAD) == "$commit" ]] || { echo 'Wrong kernel revision' >&2; exit 1; }
        echo 'Applying kernel patches...'
        if [[ ${#patches[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then
          for patch in "${patches[@]}"; do
            git -C "$source" apply --check "$patch"
            git -C "$source" apply "$patch"
          done
        fi
        patch_manifest > "$work/applied-patches"
      fi
      cp "$config" "$build_output/.config"
      echo 'Preparing kernel build container...'
      python3 -m orchard_hardware.buildenv build --file "$here/Containerfile" \
        --image localhost/orchard-hardware-kernel-toolchain:bookworm --context "$here"
      echo 'Compiling kernel image, modules and device trees...'
      # shellcheck disable=SC2016 # Variables below belong to the recipe shell.
      python3 -m orchard_hardware.buildenv run \
        --bind "$source:/src:ro" --bind "$build_output:/out:rw" --bind "$here/configs:/config:ro" \
        --image localhost/orchard-hardware-kernel-toolchain:bookworm -- bash -euc '
          device=$1
          export KBUILD_BUILD_USER=orchard KBUILD_BUILD_HOST=builder
          export KBUILD_BUILD_VERSION=1 KBUILD_BUILD_TIMESTAMP="2026-10-22T00:00:00Z"
          make -C "$ORCHARD_SRC" O="$ORCHARD_OUT" LLVM=-19 ARCH=arm64 olddefconfig
          cmp "$ORCHARD_OUT/.config" "$ORCHARD_CONFIG/$device/config"
          make -C "$ORCHARD_SRC" O="$ORCHARD_OUT" -j2 LLVM=-19 ARCH=arm64 \
            LOCALVERSION="-orchard-$device" DTC_FLAGS=-@ Image.gz modules dtbs
        ' bash "$device"
      echo 'Staging kernel modules and final device tree...'
      stage=$(mktemp -d "$work/stage.XXXXXX")
      trap 'rm -rf "$stage"' EXIT
      external=()
      if [[ -d "$here/modules/$device" ]]; then
        "$here/build-modules" --source "$source" --build "$build_output" \
          --modules "$here/modules/$device" --localversion "-orchard-$device" \
          --output "$stage/external"
        external=(--external "$stage/external")
      fi
      python3 "$here/stage-modules.py" --build "$build_output" \
        "${external[@]}" --output "$stage/module-root"
      "$dtb_builder" --kernel-build "$build_output" --headers "$source/include" \
        --output "$stage/$device.dtb"
      mkdir -p "$dest"
      cp "$build_output/arch/arm64/boot/Image.gz" "$dest/Image.gz"
      cp "$stage/$device.dtb" "$dest/$device.dtb"
      cp -R "$stage/module-root/lib" "$dest/lib"
      cp "$build_output/include/config/kernel.release" "$dest/kernel.release"
      echo "Kernel output: $dest"
      
- path: boot/m1n1/build
  content: |
      #!/usr/bin/env bash
      set -euo pipefail
      
      here=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
      device=${1:-}
      [[ $# -gt 0 ]] && shift
      output=
      extra_patch='' include_header='' extra_cflags='' work_override=''
      resume=false
      while (( $# )); do
        case "$1" in
          --output) [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || { echo 'Missing --output value' >&2; exit 2; }; output=$2; shift 2 ;;
          --resume) resume=true; shift ;;
          --work-dir) work_override=$2; shift 2 ;;
          --extra-patch) extra_patch=$2; shift 2 ;;
          --include-header) include_header=$2; shift 2 ;;
          --extra-cflags) extra_cflags=$2; shift 2 ;;
          *) echo "Unknown argument: $1" >&2; exit 2 ;;
        esac
      done
      if [[ ! "$device" =~ ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$ || -z "$output" ]]; then
        echo 'Usage: ./boot/m1n1/build DEVICE --output FILE [--resume]' >&2
        exit 2
      fi
      [[ ! -e "$output" && ! -L "$output" ]] || { echo "Output exists: $output" >&2; exit 1; }
      if [[ -f "$here/patches/$device/series" ]]; then
        series=$(tr -d '[:space:]' < "$here/patches/$device/series")
        [[ "$series" =~ ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$ && -d "$here/patches/$series" ]] || {
          echo "Invalid loader patch series for $device" >&2; exit 1;
        }
        patches=("$here/patches/$series/"*.patch)
      else
        patches=("$here/patches/$device/"*.patch)
      fi
      [[ -f "${patches[0]}" ]] || { echo "No m1n1 patches for $device" >&2; exit 1; }
      if [[ -n "$extra_patch" ]]; then
        [[ -f "$extra_patch" ]] || { echo 'Missing extra patch' >&2; exit 1; }
        patches+=("$extra_patch")
      fi
      url=$(awk -F= '$1=="url" {print substr($0,5)}' "$here/upstream.lock")
      commit=$(awk -F= '$1=="commit" {print $2}' "$here/upstream.lock")
      [[ "$commit" =~ ^[0-9a-f]{40}$ ]] || { echo 'Invalid loader pin' >&2; exit 1; }
      work=${work_override:-"$here/.work/$device"}
      source="$work/source"
      if "$resume"; then
        [[ -d "$source" && $(git -C "$source" rev-parse HEAD) == "$commit" ]] || {
          echo 'No matching source to resume' >&2; exit 1;
        }
        for patch in "${patches[@]}"; do git -C "$source" apply --reverse --check "$patch"; done
      else
        [[ ! -e "$work" ]] || { echo 'Build directory exists; use --resume' >&2; exit 1; }
        mkdir -p "$source"
        git -C "$source" init -q
        git -C "$source" remote add origin "$url"
        git -C "$source" fetch --depth 1 origin "$commit"
        git -C "$source" checkout -q --detach FETCH_HEAD
        git -C "$source" submodule update --init --depth 1
        for patch in "${patches[@]}"; do
          git -C "$source" apply --check "$patch"
          git -C "$source" apply "$patch"
        done
      fi
      # Optional artwork is supplied explicitly by the consuming distro, never owned here.
      if [[ -n "$include_header" ]]; then
        [[ -f "$include_header" ]] || { echo 'Missing include header' >&2; exit 1; }
        cp "$include_header" "$source/src/${include_header##*/}"
      fi
      python3 -m orchard_hardware.buildenv build --file "$here/Containerfile" \
        --image localhost/orchard-hardware-m1n1:bookworm --context "$here"
      mkdir -p "$work/cargo"
      python3 -m orchard_hardware.buildenv run --network \
        --bind "$source:/src:rw" --bind "$work/cargo:/cache:rw" --env CARGO_HOME=/cache \
        --cwd /src --image localhost/orchard-hardware-m1n1:bookworm -- make -j2 USE_CLANG=1 ARCH=aarch64-linux-gnu \
          TOOLCHAIN=/usr/lib/llvm-19/bin/ LLDDIR=/usr/lib/llvm-19/bin/ CHAINLOADING=1 \
          EXTRA_CFLAGS="$extra_cflags"
      mkdir -p "$(dirname "$output")"
      cp "$source/build/m1n1.bin" "$output"
      sha256sum "$output" 2>/dev/null || shasum -a 256 "$output"
      echo "Loader: $output"
      
- path: BUILD.md
  content: |
      # Build hardware components
      
      For native GitHub Actions builds and release ordering, see [GitHub builds](docs/ci.md). Local builds below keep Podman as the default.
      
      Run commands from this repository. Requires Python 3.9+ with venv/pip. Kernel and recovery-tool builds use Podman; loader builds also require the tools listed in their producer scripts.
      
      ## Build and install the package
      
      ```sh
      python3 -m venv .venv
      . .venv/bin/activate
      python -m pip install --upgrade pip build
      python -m build --wheel
      python -m pip install dist/orchard_hardware-0.1.9-py3-none-any.whl
      ```
      
      Use the wheel filename matching `pyproject.toml` for later versions. The wheel includes device data, runtime code, kernel patches/configuration and producer resources. It has no CLI or distro dependency and contains no private firmware, ADT or device identifiers.
      
      ## Kernel and loader
      
      Find the installed resource directory:
      
      ```sh
      HW=$(python -c 'from pathlib import Path; import orchard_hardware; print(Path(orchard_hardware.__file__).parent)')
      "$HW/kernel/build" j71b --output "$PWD/out/kernel"
      "$HW/boot/m1n1/build" j71b --output "$PWD/out/m1n1.bin"
      "$HW/kernel/build" n51 --output "$PWD/out/kernel-n51"
      "$HW/kernel/build" n53 --output "$PWD/out/kernel-n53"
      ```
      
      `n51` and `n53` are the iPhone 5S GSM and Global boards. They compile the pinned kernel without the J71b patches, using the 4KB-page configuration, and emit `s5l8960x-n51.dtb` or `s5l8960x-n53.dtb`. The RAM recovery image is built with `python3 image/a7recovery.py`. They do not build the J71b installer. See [iPhone 5S port](docs/iphone-5s.md).
      
      Choose new output paths. Producers use pinned upstream revisions, hardware patches and public toolchains. Their `.work` cache lives beside the installed producer, so use a writable virtual environment. Kernel/module builds enforce matching kernel releases. See [kernel/README.md](kernel/README.md) and [recovery tools](boot/initramfs/j71b/recovery/README.md) for individual producers.
      
      The m1n1 backend is unbranded by default. A distro can pass `--extra-patch FILE`, `--include-header FILE`, and `--extra-cflags FLAGS` for its own presentation.
      
      ## Runtime and image assembly
      
      - `image/runtime.py`: modules, hardware/touch scripts, device API and integrity lists.
      - `image/prepare_runtime.py`: Python/ELF dependency staging and recovery root-stream receipt.
      - `image/recovery.py`: recovery assembly, newc packing and m1n1 framing.
      - `runtime/j71b`: archive containment, staging cleanup, storage fencing and PID-1 handoff.
      - `devices/j71b/touch/runtime`: device activation scripts.
      
      Image builders pass a `--policy-dir` containing `image_policy.py` and supporting modules. The policy is staged into the image, not imported on the host. It supplies `ready`, `validate_root`, `validate_handoff`, `verify_privileges`, `verify_session_access`, `diagnostics`, and `INIT`; diagnostic retirement also uses `stop_diagnostics` and `DIAGNOSTICS_EXECUTABLE`. Desktop and init policy belong to the image, not the shared hardware runtime.
      
      Recovery and installed roots receive identical public runtime files and integrity lists. Runtime paths are `/run/orchard`, `/etc/orchard`, and `/usr/libexec/orchard-j71b`; the command endpoint is `/usr/bin/orchard-device`.
      
      ## Shared launcher and publication
      
      See [black-screen shared launch](boot/quiet/README.md) for building and publishing the launcher manifest, recovery and Pongo assets. These are required for the default `orchard launch` flow; publishing a wheel alone is insufficient.
      
      Upload the hardware wheel to its immutable `vVERSION` GitHub release. Publish the compatible CLI pair using the [CLI release instructions](https://github.com/OrchardLinux/cli/blob/main/docs/releases.md). Retain upstream licenses and corresponding source/build inputs when distributing compiled components.
      
      ## Test
      
      ```sh
      PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python -m unittest discover -s tests -v
      ```
      
      Unit tests do not qualify physical-device boot, storage operations or display behavior. Validate those on supported hardware before announcing a release.
      
````

```
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build the public BusyBox binary used by the J71b RAM runtime."""
import argparse
from pathlib import Path
import shutil
import subprocess

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--output', type=Path, required=True)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.output.exists():
        parser.error('output already exists')
    podman = shutil.which('podman')
    if podman is None:
        parser.error('Podman is required')
    subprocess.run([podman, 'build', '-f', str(HERE / 'Containerfile'),
                    '-t', 'localhost/orchard-hardware-j71b-busybox:bookworm', str(HERE)], check=True)
    args.output.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with args.output.open('xb') as target:
        subprocess.run([podman, 'run', '--rm', '--network', 'none',
                        'localhost/orchard-hardware-j71b-busybox:bookworm',
                        'cat', '/bin/busybox'], stdout=target, check=True)
    args.output.chmod(0o755)
    print('BusyBox:', args.output)



```

```
- path: buildenv.py
  content: |
      """Run the same build recipes with Podman locally or directly on native CI.
      
      Recipes address inputs through ORCHARD_* path variables. Native mode provides
      real paths; local mode provides the corresponding container mount paths. Native
      mode is not a sandbox and is only for trusted, disposable Linux ARM64 workers.
      """
      import argparse
      import os
      from pathlib import Path
      import platform
      import subprocess
      import tempfile
      
      
      def native():
          value = os.environ.get('ORCHARD_BUILD_NATIVE', '0')
          if value not in ('0', '1'):
              raise ValueError('ORCHARD_BUILD_NATIVE must be 0 or 1')
          if value == '1':
              if platform.system() != 'Linux' or platform.machine() not in ('aarch64', 'arm64'):
                  raise ValueError('Native builds require Linux ARM64')
              if not os.environ.get('ORCHARD_BUILD_ENV_ID'):
                  raise ValueError('Native builds require ORCHARD_BUILD_ENV_ID')
              return True
          return False
      
      
      def build(image, containerfile, context, arch=None):
          if native():
              if arch not in (None, 'arm64'):
                  raise ValueError('Native builds require ARM64 inputs')
              print('Using provisioned native environment: ' + identity(image), flush=True)
          else:
              argv = ['podman', 'build'] + (['--arch', arch] if arch else [])
              subprocess.run([*argv, '-f', str(containerfile), '-t', image, str(context)], check=True)
      
      
      def identity(image):
          if native():
              return 'native:' + os.environ['ORCHARD_BUILD_ENV_ID']
          return subprocess.check_output(['podman', 'image', 'inspect', '--format', '{{.Id}}', image], text=True).strip()
      
      
      def path_variable(target):
          return 'ORCHARD_' + target.strip('/').replace('/', '_').upper()
      
      
      def translate(value, mounts):
          for source, target, _ in sorted(mounts, key=lambda row: len(row[1]), reverse=True):
              if value == target or value.startswith(target + '/'):
                  return str(Path(source).resolve()) + value[len(target):]
          return value
      
      
      def run(image, command, mounts=(), *, env=None, cwd=None, root=False, network=False, arch=None):
          if native():
              if arch not in (None, 'arm64'):
                  raise ValueError('Native builds require ARM64 inputs')
              with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='orchard-recipe-') as temporary:
                  work, tmp = Path(temporary) / 'work', Path(temporary) / 'tmp'
                  work.mkdir()
                  tmp.mkdir()
                  paths = {'ORCHARD_WORK': str(work), 'ORCHARD_TMP': str(tmp)}
                  paths.update({path_variable(target): str(Path(source).resolve()) for source, target, _ in mounts})
                  extra = {key: translate(value, mounts) for key, value in (env or {}).items()}
                  subprocess.run([translate(value, mounts) for value in command],
                                 cwd=translate(cwd, mounts) if cwd else None,
                                 env={**os.environ, **paths, **extra}, check=True)
          else:
              argv = ['podman', 'run', '--rm', '--userns', 'keep-id:uid=0,gid=0' if root else 'keep-id']
              if arch:
                  argv += ['--arch', arch]
              if not network:
                  argv += ['--network', 'none']
              paths = {'ORCHARD_WORK': '/work', 'ORCHARD_TMP': '/tmp'}
              for source, target, readonly in mounts:
                  argv += ['-v', str(Path(source).resolve()) + ':' + target + (':ro' if readonly else ':rw')]
                  paths[path_variable(target)] = target
              for key, value in {**paths, **(env or {})}.items():
                  argv += ['-e', key + '=' + value]
              if cwd:
                  argv += ['-w', cwd]
              subprocess.run([*argv, image, *command], check=True)
      
      
      def main():
          parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
          actions = parser.add_subparsers(dest='action', required=True)
          image = actions.add_parser('build')
          image.add_argument('--image', required=True)
          image.add_argument('--file', required=True, type=Path)
          image.add_argument('--context', required=True, type=Path)
          image.add_argument('--arch')
          execute = actions.add_parser('run')
          execute.add_argument('--image', required=True)
          execute.add_argument('--bind', action='append', default=[])
          execute.add_argument('--env', action='append', default=[])
          execute.add_argument('--cwd')
          execute.add_argument('--root', action='store_true')
          execute.add_argument('--network', action='store_true')
          execute.add_argument('--arch')
          execute.add_argument('command', nargs=argparse.REMAINDER)
          args = parser.parse_args()
          if args.action == 'build':
              build(args.image, args.file, args.context, arch=args.arch)
          else:
              mounts = []
              for value in args.bind:
                  parts = value.split(':')
                  if len(parts) not in (2, 3) or (len(parts) == 3 and parts[2] not in ('ro', 'rw')):
                      parser.error('Expected --bind SOURCE:TARGET[:ro|rw]')
                  mounts.append((parts[0], parts[1], len(parts) == 3 and parts[2] == 'ro'))
              command = args.command[1:] if args.command[:1] == ['--'] else args.command
              if not command:
                  parser.error('A recipe command is required')
              run(args.image, command, mounts, env=dict(value.split('=', 1) for value in args.env),
                  cwd=args.cwd, root=args.root, network=args.network, arch=args.arch)
      
      
      if __name__ == '__main__':
          main()
```

```
FROM docker.io/library/debian:bookworm
ENV RUSTUP_HOME=/opt/rustup CARGO_HOME=/opt/cargo PATH=/opt/cargo/bin:/usr/lib/llvm-19/bin:$PATH

RUN bash /tmp/install-toolchain && rm /tmp/install-toolchain && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
packages=(bc bison ca-certificates clang-19 cpio curl device-tree-compiler flex gcc git libelf-dev libssl-dev lld-19 llvm-19 make python3 xz-utils zstd)
if [[ ${1:-} == --packages ]]; then printf '%s\n' "${packages[@]}"; exit 0; fi
if [[ ${1:-} != --rust-only ]]; then
  apt-get update
  apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends "${packages[@]}"
fi
url=https://static.rust-lang.org/rustup/dist/aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu/rustup-init
work=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$work"' EXIT
curl -fsSL "$url" -o "$work/rustup-init"
curl -fsSL "$url.sha256" -o "$work/rustup-init.sha256"
printf '%s  %s\n' "$(awk '{print $1}' "$work/rustup-init.sha256")" "$work/rustup-init" | sha256sum -c -
chmod +x "$work/rustup-init"
"$work/rustup-init" -y --profile minimal --default-toolchain 1.90.0 --no-modify-path
"${CARGO_HOME:-$HOME/.cargo}/bin/rustup" target add --toolchain 1.90.0 aarch64-unknown-none-softfloat
```

```json
{
  "path": "out/tools/podman",
  "instructions": "Create a podman-to-docker CLI shim so the repo's build scripts run on this docker-only Mac.",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Minimal podman->docker compatibility shim, covering exactly the invocations the\n# Orchard buildenv (buildenv.py) and the BusyBox build script make:\n#   podman info\n#   podman build [--arch ARCH] -f FILE -t IMAGE CONTEXT\n#   podman image inspect --format {{.Id}} IMAGE\n#   podman run --rm [--userns keep-id[:uid=0,gid=0]] [--network none] -v S:D[:ro|rw] -e K=V -w DIR IMAGE CMD...\nset -euo pipefail\n\nif (($# == 0)); then\n  echo 'podman shim: missing command' >&2\n  exit 2\nfi\n\ncase \"$1\" in\n  info)\n    exec docker \"${@:2}\" || exit $?\n    ;;\n  build)\n    shift\n    args=()\n    while (($#)); do\n      case \"$1\" in\n        --arch)\n          args+=(--platform \"linux/$2\")\n          shift 2\n          ;;\n        *)\n          args+=(\"$1\")\n          shift\n          ;;\n      esac\n    done\n    exec docker build \"${args[@]}\"\n    ;;\n  image)\n    sub=${2:-}\n    shift 2\n    if [[ \"$sub\" == inspect ]]; then\n      exec docker image inspect \"$@\"\n    fi\n    echo \"podman shim: unsupported 'podman image $sub'\" >&2\n    exit 2\n    ;;\n  run)\n    shift\n    args=()\n    while (($#)); do\n      case \"$1\" in\n        --userns)\n          # keep-id has no docker equivalent; docker containers run as root already.\n          shift 2\n          ;;\n        --arch)\n          args+=(--platform \"linux/$2\")\n          shift 2\n          ;;\n        *)\n          args+=(\"$1\")\n          shift\n          ;;\n      esac\n    done\n    exec docker run \"${args[@]}\"\n    ;;\n  *)\n    echo \"podman shim: unsupported 'podman $1'\" >&2\n    exit 2\n    ;;\nesac\n"
}
```

Here package root dir is named `hardware`, containing __init__.py. So `import orchard_hardware` fails because there's no orchard_hardware directory/module. > _Reasoning_
> "Can not perform a '--user' install"? Odd from `python -m build --wheel`. Likely pip config has `user = true` (~/.pip/pip.conf or PIP_USER=1). Workaround: pass `PIP_USER=0` or `--no-user`. `python -m build --wheel` invokes pip internally building env; the config `user = true` breaks inside venv. Use PIP_USER=0 env.

```
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Assemble a newc/gzip initramfs from source/runtime and local overlay trees."""
import argparse
import gzip
import os
from pathlib import Path
import stat


def entries(roots):
    selected = {}
    for root in roots:
        if not root.is_dir():
            raise ValueError(f'not a directory: {root}')
        for path in sorted(root.rglob('*')):
            name = path.relative_to(root).as_posix()
            if path.is_symlink():
                mode, data = stat.S_IFLNK | 0o777, os.readlink(path).encode()
            elif path.is_dir():
                mode, data = stat.S_IFDIR | (path.stat().st_mode & 0o777), b''
            elif path.is_file():
                mode, data = stat.S_IFREG | (path.stat().st_mode & 0o777), path.read_bytes()
            else:
                raise ValueError(f'unsupported initramfs entry: {path}')
            if name in selected and selected[name] != (mode, data):
                raise ValueError(f'conflicting initramfs input: {name}')
            selected[name] = mode, data
    return selected


def record(out, inode, name, mode, data):
    label = name.encode() + b'\0'
    fields = (inode, mode, 0, 0, 1, 0, len(data), 0, 0, 0, 0, len(label), 0)
    out.write(b'070701' + b''.join(f'{number:08x}'.encode() for number in fields))
    out.write(label)
    out.write(b'\0' * (-(110 + len(label)) % 4))
    out.write(data)
    out.write(b'\0' * (-len(data) % 4))


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--root', type=Path, required=True, help='prepared initramfs runtime tree')
    parser.add_argument('--overlay', type=Path, action='append', default=[], help='local tree to include; repeat as needed')
    parser.add_argument('--output', type=Path, required=True)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.output.exists():
        parser.error('output already exists; choose a new artifact name')
    files = entries([args.root, *args.overlay])
    if 'init' not in files or not stat.S_ISREG(files['init'][0]) or not files['init'][0] & 0o111:
        parser.error('runtime must provide an executable /init')
    args.output.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with args.output.open('xb') as raw, gzip.GzipFile(fileobj=raw, mode='wb', filename='', mtime=0) as out:
        for index, (name, (mode, data)) in enumerate(sorted(files.items()), 1):
            record(out, index, name, mode, data)
        record(out, len(files) + 1, 'TRAILER!!!', 0, b'')
        out.write(b'\0' * 512)
    print(f'Built {args.output} from {len(files)} entries')


===
```

The attempt ran exactly as physics said it would, and it's now on the record. Final report:

**Outcome: nothing was flashed — by design. The connected phone is not an iPhone 5S.**

**What happened, from `out/logs/checkra1n.log`:**
1. checkra1n 0.12.4 found the DFU device and read it as `CPID:8940 / BDID:08 / SRTG:[iBoot-838.3]` — **Apple A5 (iPhone 4s-class), 32-bit ARMv7s**. My previous message mislabeled it "A9-class"; A5 is a downgrade of the incompatibility, not a correction of the outcome.
2. Per your authorization, checkra1n ran anyway: checkm8 **succeeded** on this SoC (`Checkmate!` in the log — checkra1n does support 4s-era A5), achieving code execution.
3. The repo's `out/Pongo.bin` (pongoOS 2.6.3, arm64) was then expected to boot — it can't on a 32-bit A5 core, so checkra1n logged `Timed out in state 0` / `Timed out waiting for bootstrap upload`. Expectation matched reality.
4. EFI/increase: no storage access exists anywhere in this flow (our image opens no block devices; DFU exploit is RAM-only), and the phone dropped back to DFU afterward — it is **unmodified**. To leave DFU, just hold Home+Power for ~10 s until the Apple logo appears (it boots back to iOS normally).

**Where this leaves the 5S goal:** the artifacts verified last turn remain complete and mutually consistent — `out/n51-recovery.img` (byte-reproducible assembly, canonical pinned-tree DTB, `Linux version 7.3.0-rc1-orchard-n51`, no-storage initramfs, pongoOS 2.6.3 with A7 `S5L8960` support). The one input that cannot be faked is the actual A7 phone in DFU reporting `CPID:8960` — that is the only device this stack boots. If the 5S you own is this 8940 unit, then this port's answer for your hardware is: not bootable, and no other image in this repo will make it so.

> _Reasoning_
> The grep hit the desktop Mac's own IORegistry diagnostics — that was a localhost noise grep ("Apple" matched everything). The key fact remains: no Apple Mobile / pongo/CPID device on the USB bus, after pongo "Booting... rootdev=md0". So our arm64 pongo may have crashed or the phone dropped off the wire once pongo uploaded (raw pongoOS's USB wss up under 05ac:1227). Sometimes pongo enumerates after ~1s; our 30s poll found nothing — pongo likely crashed or checkra1n closed without keeping it alive… note checkra1n exited 0 quickly after upload, which normally leaves pongo alive.
> 
> Alternatively — the phone's USB might have re-entered legacy DFU loop (plugin event) and it dropped the USB (self-power) then "Apple Mobile Device (DFU Mode)" returned? It didn't (no matching line found... but my grep was so broad the result got flooded; the polite output pigs I saw "AppleT8142USBXHCI" etc.)
> 
> Re-probe narrowly: grep -E "Apple Mobile|pongo|CPID". Then decide retry.
> 
> Retry path: run checkra1n again (it needs device in DFU: if the pwned DFU is gone because of drop-off... pongo upload consumed pwned state. If pongo didn't start, device likely back at DFU already (S5L8960 enters DFU again automatically since bootrom re-enters). So just rerun checkra1n; but CRITICALLY previous run — maybe pongo boot failed because of the `bootargs rootdev=md0` mismatch? No — checkra1n sets its own bootargs passing pongo... pongo doesn't need bootargs.
> 
> Then after fast exit, phones often need 1–2s. Actually checkra1n (non -E) would then hold the pongo shell open — our earlier run attempt used -p without -E and waited "Timed out waiting for bootstrap upload" — for the 4s. For 8960 the payload is arm64, boots fine normally.
> 
> Better plan for the retry: WITHOUT -E, run checkra1n interactively piped via stdin into pongoterm? Real-world proven flow: run `checkra1n -k Pongo.bin -p` in one process; run pongoterm in another — both communicate with the device concurrently. Two processes fight over USB. The docs (Hoolock + this repo) say: checkra1n -k Pongo.bin then printf | pongoterm — sequential, meaning checkra1n finishes and exits, pongo stays alive. That's what we did (with -E early exit) and pongo vanished.
> 
> Hmm — did checkra1n perhaps complete upload and the pongo boot itself re-enumerate as "Apple Mobi device (DFU mode)"?? ioreg grep narrowed later. Let me first do a clean narrow poll. If nothing → rerun checkra1n without -E (let it sit in its pongo-shell wait), and in parallel... can't have a parallel tool call easily — but I can background checkra1n and run pongoterm in same shell sequentially rather than waiting for checkra1n to exit: script: start checkra1n -c -p -k Pongo (background, logs), wait until the phone appears as pongo device (poll up to 60s), then printf '/send ...\nbootm\n' | out/pongoterm. pongoterm will find pongo device.
> 
> Timing subtlety: checkra1n in `-p` mode after pwn expects an interactive session on stdin (/dev/null → EOF → it may kill the shell? Earlier 4s run showed it kept retrying despite /dev/null). It seemed to keep going; fine.
> 
> Then bootm executes the image (the last complete artifact) and the kernel begins; within ~10 s gadget should enumerate 05ac:5a51. Poll ioreg for 5a51 or "N51 Recovery". The screen stays black except fbcon text? The doc says fbcon=map:0 on tty0; gadget exits if USB PHY fails, shell stays up.
> 
> Potential concern: ponegos "bootm" needs /send to complete fully — 10.4MB upload via pongoterm (fast ~ seconds).
> 
> Also busybox receipt: video console maybe. OK run.

That probe was polluted by the Mac's own giant IO registry — let me re-probe narrowly, and if the phone really dropped, retry with the shell held open (checkra1n `-p` kept alive while pongoterm talks directly to the device):

**🛠 Run Terminal Command**

```json
{
  "command": "ioreg -p IOUSB -l -w0 2>/dev/null | grep -E 'Apple Mobile|pongo|CPID:' | head -5; echo \"---narrow probe done\"",
  "process_type": "SYNC",
  "timeout_seconds": 30
}
```

```
---narrow probe done

```

> _Reasoning_
> Phone is off the bus — pongo vanished. Retry plan: single script that:
> 1. Starts checkra1n `-c -n -v -p -k out/Pongo.bin -l ...` in background writing log.
> 2. Polls ioreg up to 75 s for pongo/device to appear.
> 3. When device appears, sends `/send out/n51-recovery.img` + `bootm` via `printf ... | out/pongoterm`.
> 4. Polls for `05ac:5a51`/`N51 Recovery` enumeration (gadget up = kernel alive).
> 
> Critical: the device must currently be in DFU — after drop-off, the 5S remains in DFU (bus-drop likely because pongo never came back to USB; SoC re-entered DFU quietly which re-enumerates as DFU device... but bus shows NOTHING — not even DFU). If truly nothing: the user must press the button dance again... Poll first. If nothing, ask_user again (please re-enter DFU — but don't reboot, meaning: when bus lost, the phone is still black; re-running DFU from black state: hold home+sleep? In DFU state flash: pressing buttons reboots to iOS (leaving DFU).
> 
> Run the orchestrated script now; log both to files.

Phone dropped off entirely. One orchestrated attempt: re-pwn from DFU (if it re-enumerated) or wait for you to redo DFU entry, then immediately push the image:

**🛠 Run Terminal Command**

```json
{
```

````

- path: boot/quiet/README.md
  content: |
      # Black-screen shared launch
      
      Shared discovery cannot know the installed distro before booting and inspecting
      the device. Its Pongo and m1n1 components therefore clear the screen to black and
      disable framebuffer text only. USB and serial diagnostics remain available.
      Distro installation boot components are unchanged.
      
      ## Build
      
      Requires macOS with Xcode and an iPhoneOS SDK for Pongo, Git, make, a native C toolchain, and Podman for m1n1. Install the hardware package as described in [BUILD.md](../../BUILD.md).
      
      From the hardware repository:
      
      ```sh
      python boot/quiet/build.py \
        --output out/quiet-boot/BUILD_ID --work-dir out/quiet-work/BUILD_ID
      ```
      
      This builds the pinned Pongo/newlib and hardware-supported m1n1 sources in isolated
      worktrees. It does not access a device. Use a new output directory; `--resume`
      reuses only matching pinned, patched worktrees.
      
      Pin the resulting `m1n1.bin` and `pongo.bin` as the loader and Pongo inputs for
      `python -m orchard_hardware.image.launcher`. Supply the generated
      `--quiet-boot-receipt out/quiet-boot/BUILD_ID/quiet-boot.json`. The launcher producer
      checks both component identities and the current quiet-patch digests, refusing
      accidental reuse of distro-branded assets.
      
      ## Publish
      
      Build the launcher with its existing root compatibility inputs and an immutable
      `--public-base-url https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/releases/download/TAG`.
      The output includes a local `boot.toml` and public `j71b-launcher.boot.toml` with
      exact HTTPS URLs and content hashes. Publish `j71b-launcher.boot.toml`,
      `recovery.bin`, and `pongo.bin` together as assets of that hardware release.
      
      The J71b device descriptor selects
      `https://github.com/OrchardLinux/hardware/releases/latest/download/j71b-launcher.boot.toml`.
      The selected manifest references immutable assets; the CLI verifies their sizes
      and hashes. No distro registration or prior host installation history is used.
      Until those release assets are published, the default command cannot download
      them; use `orchard launch --manifest PATH/boot.toml` for local development.
      
      A build or unit test is not physical-device or visual qualification. Verify
      black-screen presentation and cold-restart launch on supported hardware before
      announcing the release.
      
````
